# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-02-01_TotalExtraction_67788302.pdf
  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 11, 2019 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead met in regular session on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne (arriving at 6:06 p.m.), Scott Roybal and Dicky Shanor. Absent: Jeff White. Also present: 
Kris Jones, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna. Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 28, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mr. Roybal declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy 
Dodge, Inc.) and vacated council chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as 
presented, seconded by Mr. Case. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to 
Chambers. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for Lot 4, Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail 
Park Third Filing, and a Portion of adjacent Tundra Drive right-of-way, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of Tundra Drive and Clear Creek Parkway). Tawn 
Hillenbrand, Planning and Development Department, advised the annexation request was 
submitted by the landowner, provided information on the property and reviewed requirements of 
and compliance with annexation conditions contained in state statutes.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
   
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for Lots 6 and 7, Block 1, and all that 
16’ alley located in Block 1, except east 8’ adjacent to Lots 3 and 4, Block 1 Countryside Addition, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southwest intersection of Countryside Avenue and 
Whitney Road).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, advised the applicant and 
City staff feel the annexation is not ready to proceed at this time, noting the ordinance has not been 
submitted to the City. Mr. Lloyd advised a second public hearing will be held on March 11, 2019, 

  
and noted it is anticipated the annexation will be considered at that time.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Creating Chapter 12.28, Downtown Graffiti Abatement 
Pilot Program, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and providing procedures for the operation thereof. Vicki Dugger, 
Executive Director, Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority, spoke in support of the 
ordinance, addressed a concern related to distributing information to property owners and advised 
the DDA has hired help to obtain property owner information from the Secretary of State’s office 
which will be maintained in a database.  She provided ownership information on properties in the 
downtown district, stating 28% of owners are absentee. Ms. Dugger explained letters will be sent 
to owners giving the option to “opt-out” of the program. Ms. Dugger advised, upon inquiry, 
program funds will be taken out of the mill levy budget line item.  Upon inquiry by Richard 
Johnson, Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, advised property owners will not receive citations 
for graffiti.  Upon inquiry related to the course of action for those who “opt-out” and are affected 
by graffiti, Mayor Orr explained Code Enforcement may become involved.  It was noted the 
program is a two-year pilot program. Mr. Johnson discussed graffiti culture and suggestions 
provided by downtown property owners including public education and an area where graffiti is 
allowed.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dugger explained initial letters were sent 
through registered mail.  Discussion followed and included: Proposed graffiti removal process, 
methods utilized to communicate with property owners and the importance of an updated property 
ownership database. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mr. Shanor voting “no”. (#4259) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Delegating the review authority authorized by W.S. §34-
12-103(b) to the Director of the City of Cheyenne Planning and Development Department.  Charles 
Bloom, Planning and Development Director, advised the County determined they will not enter 
into an MOU at this time and explained the review process going forward noting the City’s ability 
to comment on County plats and the review process does apply to stormwater.  It was reported that 
County Commissioners are required to submit written notice if they disagree with City comments.  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Discussion topics 
included: Concerns related to the ordinance and the way state statute is written, potential for future 
annexation requests that involve land that hasn’t met City standards, possible impact of not 
approving the ordinance and future discussions with legislature on the topic. Amy Allen, City 
Engineer, clarified when annexations occur, City standards must be met.  Motion carried. Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne and 
Mr. Roybal voting “no”. (#4260)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development 
Regulating Plan and Section 5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC) amending Ordinance Number 3741, which established The 
Village Planned Unit Development (PUD), by adding indoor entertainment as a land use category 
permitted by right for Lot 28 of Block 2 of The Village PUD, adding a new Section 5.11 to defer 
to the UDC standards where the PUD is silent on Development Standards, and, pursuant to Section 

  
2.2.1 of the UDC, amending The Village PUD map to reflect Lot 28 instead of Lots 30 and 31 of 
The Village Subdivision for the subject parcel, Cheyenne, Wyoming, (located west of and adjacent 
to Powderhouse Road, south of Old Town Lane). Eric Nelson, Principal Owner, Wyoming Downs, 
provided history of Wyoming Downs business operations including community tax revenues, 
noted average age of customers, explained the company’s interest in the proposed location, 
provided information on calls responded to by Police Department at their existing location (report 
on file with Clerk’s office) and stated their first priority is customer and employee safety.  Mr. 
Nelson discussed a future open house to be held at the proposed location to provide public 
information and, upon inquiry, explained the difference between gambling and pari-mutuel 
waging.  He advised it is his understanding that other nearby and adjacent properties are correctly 
zoned for indoor entertainment. Joe Svec, #1 Properties, explained the process of acquiring the 
property and reported beginning in 2018, signatures were collected for the zone change petition 
from nearby property owners and provided information relating to petition requirements. Upon 
inquiry, Mr. Svec advised the former Jackson’s building has been vacant for 2 years with minimal 
interest in the building.  Eric Cook, Chief Operations Officer, Wyoming Downs, provided and 
reviewed handouts (on file with Clerk’s office) discussing: Business customer base, proposed 
restaurant environment including alcohol and reported a recent decline in business at the existing 
location.  Upon request for clarification relating to a letter included in the handout, Mr. Cook 
confirmed the letter is undated and stated it was prepared on November 9, 2018.  Mr. Cook 
provided additional information relating to desire to expand, details of the proposed building 
layout and operation, incident report involving Police Department calls for service at exiting 
location, projected tax revenues, and potential for ordinance postponement to allow for additional 
public input at a future open house.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Nelson explained physical altercations 
occurred outside of the facility according to an internal control report.  Speaking in opposition of 
the ordinance were:  Mike Konar, Frank Cole, Evan Asher, Paul Wood, Scott Weaver, Gloria 
Milmont, Laramie Ringolsby, Maresa Hilton, Candis Pickard, CRMC Davis Hospice Center, 
Crystal Lewis, Trisha Smedley, Bryan Young, Joe Fiedler, Holly Fiedler and Kim and Conner 
Shumway.  Comments and concerns discussed included: Proposed location proximity to homes, 
churches, schools, daycares, hospice center, and other businesses, notification to nearby property 
owners required for zone change, incidents occurring in the existing location parking lot vs. inside 
the establishment, importance of a safe neighborhood, potential long term financial impacts 
relating to current and future residents and businesses, Police Department recommended criteria, 
data provided by Wyoming Downs, client base, future alcohol service at the proposed location, 
zone change petition process and zoning criteria. Mr. Case advised there are six lots that are 
appropriately zoned for the business directly north and northwest of the proposed location.  Donn 
Edmunds spoke in favor of a secondary location with a six-month provision.  
 
Following a brief recess, Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 8:20 p.m. Jeff Dierksen spoke 
about neighbor’s concerns and voiced support for discontinuation of problematic behavior.  Dr. 
Rinne reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Discussion topics included: Neighborhood 
safety, community need for entertainment options, incidents at existing location isolated to the 
immediate area of the business, and potential positive financial revenue impact. Mr. Case 
distributed and discussed a zoning map of the area (on file with Clerk’s office).  Mr. Case moved 
to postpone to February 25, 2019, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting 

  
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Orr and Mr. 
Laybourn voting “no”. (City Clerk note: Dr. Rinne left the meeting due to a previous commitment 
at 8:33 p.m., prior to the vote). 
 
ORDINANCE – 1ST READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Lot 4, 
Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail Park Third Filing, and a portion of Tundra Drive, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of Tundra Drive and Clear Creek Parkway) – referred 
to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1ST READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County Swan Ranch PUD Planned 
Unit Development to City Swan Ranch PUD Planned Unit Development (Ordinance No. 3989) 
for Lot 4, Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail Park Third Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming (at the 
southwest corner of Tundra Drive and Clear Creek Parkway) – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1ST READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to LI Light 
Industrial for Hutchins Corner 2nd Filing Lots 4 & 5, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of 
and adjacent to Campstool Road, west of Hutchins Drive) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2019 budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S.  §16-4-112 & 16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#5975) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Mineral Royalty Capital 
Construction Account Grant Application to the State Loan and Investment Board on behalf of the 
Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne for the purpose of funding the finish of the second floor 
of a new Municipal Courthouse.  Ron Downey, City Treasurer, provided information on the 
amount requested and the grant application due date (2/21/2019). Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey 
confirmed the proposed grant is to finish the second floor of the new Municipal Court Building 
and advised if the grant is awarded and the second floor is not completed, half of the amount 
awarded would need to be returned.  Joe Dougherty inquired as to the specified government 
purpose of the proposed second floor space.  Mayor Orr advised the use hasn’t been determined to 
date and will be specified in the near future.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey explained project funding 
(general fund reserves and 6th Penny fund overages), current general fund amount and 60-day 
reserves requirement.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna reported there was no 
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by 
Mr. Esquibel.  Mr. Shanor abstained from discussion and voting (business affiliation with 
Wyoming State Loan and Investment Board) and vacated council chambers. Comment was made 
related to the affiliation of the resolution and the upcoming contract modification and the intended 
use for the space.  Mayor Orr advised for the purpose of the grant, specifics are not currently 

  
needed for space.  Upon inquiry, Ben Hornok, TC Solutions, explained evolving space utilization 
of the second floor involving discussions with architects and governing body input on the use of 
the second floor.  Upon inquiry related to a possible postponement of the resolution, Mr. Downey 
advised a postponement would interfere with the grant submittal deadline.  Mayor Orr explained 
her representation of the governing body before the State Loan and Investment Board and voiced 
concern about missing a grant opportunity.  Comments were made related to the resolution’s 
positive impact on the grant application and determining what will be located in the space.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Chambers. (#5976) 
 
[CA]

Contract Modification #2 to Contract #6892 between the City of Cheyenne and Noel 
Griffith Jr. & Associates Architects for Architectural and Engineering Services for finish of the 
second floor of the Cheyenne Municipal Court Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales Tax and 
General Fund reserves). Joe Dougherty commented on the elements of the project and voiced 
concern regarding costs affiliated with the project.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, 
explained the amount of the modification is approximately $95,000.  Ben Hornok, TC Solutions, 
stated a work session for utilization of the second floor will be scheduled immediately, as this is a 
time sensitive aspect of the project. Comment was made regarding determining priorities for the 
space. Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Mr. Luna moved to postpone to 
February 25, 2019, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Mr. Laybourn advised the postponement is to 
allow for further discussion related to space utilization. Mr. Hornok advised upon inquiry, that a 
postponement could potentially delay the project. Jaimie Winters, Noel Griffith & Associates, 
provided a review of the project to date, explained the only component approved for the second 
floor is the shell and provided suggestions for second floor use such as office spaces required, 
conference room space, etc.  Comments were made and included potential uses for the second 
floor and opposition of the postponement. (Clerk’s note: Dr. Rinne returned to the meeting at 9:03 
p.m.)  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” - Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Cook.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Case, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor.  Discussion 
continued relating to the following topics:  Future City department occupancy and possible 

  
renovations relating to proposed second floor of the new Municipal Court Building and the 
Municipal Building located at 2101 O’Neil Ave and status of general fund reserves.  Upon inquiry, 
Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, suggested language for amending Section II of the contract 
modification relating to second floor occupancy involving specific city departments.  Mr. 
Laybourn moved to amend Section II of the contract modification by striking the following: “The 
second floor will be designed to accommodate the following suites of offices: Mayor’s Office, 
City Attorney’s Office, and City Council” and replacing with: “The second floor will be designed 
to accommodate such offices as may be designated by Council.”, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. 
Hornok clarified that temporary housing of City departments can occur if the second floor is 
constructed as general office space if future Municipal Building renovations are made.  Motion to 
amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Comment was made 
regarding the potential expenditure of additional funds on the project and the possibility of having 
just the shell for the second floor.  Main motion as amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor 
voting “no”. (#6892) 
 
 [CA]

Appointments of Lisa Kindel, Justine Whitacre and Jared Dixon to the Mayor’s Council 
for People with Disabilities.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not 
vote (mayoral appointments).  Mayor Orr commented on the amount of applications submitted for

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 25, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in 
the City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for renewal applications 
for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, 
microbrewery, satellite distillery and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City Clerk’s 
office.   
 
Announcement was made that due to the President’s Day Holiday on Monday, February 
18, 2019, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the 
Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance 
Committee will meet Tuesday, February 19, 2019 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee 
will meet Wednesday, February 20, 2019 at Noon.   
 
Under other business: 
 
Rod Stone voiced concern with flooding issues in the Summit Drive area relating to 
development of the Thomas Heights addition and inquired on the status of previous discussions 
with City and County staff regarding initiating a review and analysis of the drainage plan from a 
third-party engineer. Upon inquiry, Mr. Stone advised he has been in contact with an engineer that 
has agreed to provide review and analysis services and confirmed the individual is not licensed in 
the State of Wyoming.  Mr. Laybourn voiced concerns relating to flooding in that area and the 
need to resolve the draining issues. 
    
Jeff Dierksen distributed information related to a recent Police Department high visibility 
patrol on Pinto Lane (on file with Clerk’s office) and upon inquiry, advised he hasn’t been in 
contact with the new City Traffic Engineer. Ms. Allen reported the Traffic Engineer has looked at 
some data concerning traffic in the area. 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 10:03. p.m.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_57293030.pdf
  
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 29, 2018 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  COUNCIL PRESIDENT – Dr. Mark Rinne; 
COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, 
Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Mayor Marian J. Orr.  Also present: Carol 
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney. In the absence of Mayor Orr, Council 
President Rinne chaired the meeting.  [City Clerk’s note:  As chair and with the exception of the 
vote taken on the main motion for Agenda Item #9, an ordinance on third and final reading, 
President Rinne exercised his option, as entitled by state statute, to not cast a vote on agenda items].  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Expression of Appreciation to the 2017-2018 Mayor’s Youth Council and State of the 
Youth Address.  Jan Spires, Youth Alternatives and staff advisor to the Youth Council, expressed 
appreciation to Youth Council members, and introduced Julia Horst, Mayor’s Youth Council chair 
for 2017-2018.  Ms. Horst presented the State of the Youth address, and provided information on 
various accomplishments, projects and programs the group participated in throughout the year. 
Ms. Spires and Eric Fountain, Mayor’s Chief of Staff, presented Certificates of Appreciation to 
the following Youth Council members: Alexis Arp, Maggie Black, McCoy Bush, Ashen Doty, 
Raegan Downham, Dylan Fertig, Julia Horst, Keiran Loughran, Emily Lucero, Nickolas Metheny, 
Kaylee Sanchez, Kenitra Shanks, Dominick Singleton and Garrett Vickery. President Rinne 
expressed appreciation to the group and their parents for their participation and support 
respectively. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Luna moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded 
by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with 
the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and President Rinne who did not vote. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 14, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on May 16, 2018.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mr. Roybal declared a conflict (business interest in voucher payable to Cowboy Dodge, 
Inc.) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as 

  
presented, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present in Council Chambers with the exception of President Rinne who did not vote.  
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Roybal returned to Council Chambers. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, provided information about the proposed 
expansion (adding an additional route) of the yard waste program, and advised she had no objection 
to not expanding the program if that was the desire of the governing body.  Ms. Nemecek, upon 
inquiry, discussed the 2014 HDR rate study and information on the yard waste program, which 
has about 375 future customers on a waiting list, and elements involved with adding one additional 
yard waste pick-up route.  Jeffrey Dierksen expressed concerns about the yard waste program and 
his belief that existing customers shouldn’t subsidize a new route. Upon inquiry by Mr. Dierksen, 
President Rinne advised the sanitation fee schedule is reviewed annually.  Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Mr. White reported there was no recommendation on the item from the Committee.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Mr. Shanor, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal, moved to amend by adding a Note 16 at the end of the schedule as 
follows: No revenues realized from the 5% sanitation fee increase may be used on payroll until 
such time as the construction and equipment objectives identified in the 2014 HDR study have 
been completed in full.  Discussion included: Construction and equipment objectives; solid waste 
enterprise fund; past fee increases; landfill expansion and closure costs; vehicle replacements and 
sanitation employee salary increases.  During discussion, Ms. Nemecek responded to questions 
involving sanitation and landfill operations, expenses and budget; anticipated future fee increases, 
and the landfill plan, and commented on the 3% pay for performance employee funds included in 
the FY2019 solid waste budget and the need to retain employees.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting 
“no” and President Rinne who did not vote.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present (including President Rinne) with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson and Mr. Luna voting “no”. (#4229) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities.  Randy 
Hays, Board of Public Utilities (BOPU), submitted a hand-out (on file with City Clerk’s office) 
which he reviewed regarding the Board’s recommendation for a 3% rate increase for both water 
and sewer funds, and explained factors involved in proposing the rate adjustments.  Mr. Hays 
provided information on how rates are assessed for multi-family vs single family residential units 
and, upon inquiry, noted the reasons for decreases in funding amounts for operational and 
maintenance, and capital expenditures affiliated with BOPU’s proposed FY2019 budget.  Jeffrey 
Dierksen questioned location identification of lead pipes relative to replacement of water mains, 
and the different rate tiers for multiple vs. single family residences with Mr. Hays providing 
information in response.  Astrid commented on fair allocation rate structures, suggesting rates be 
based upon the size of a residence, and reviewing older portions of the City to determine the 
location and replacement of lead pipes.  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes’ – all members of the governing body present with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and President Rinne who did not vote. 

  
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2018 and ending June 30, 2019. Stephanie 
Lowe, President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association, spoke in support of the proposed 3% 
employee pay for performance funding contained in the budget, noting a performance based plan 
will assist with retaining employees.  Officer Manny Fardella, Cheyenne Police Department, 
commented in support of competitive wages; consideration of needs vs. wants; 3% performance 
pay, and the value of employees.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Mr. Case moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Case moved to amend by removing 
$100,000.00 from reserves and placing it into the Abandoned Building line item, seconded by Mr. 
Cook.  Discussion included use of taxpayer’s funds vs. neighborhoods taking care of blighted 
properties through such mechanisms as property covenants; utilizing funding from recurring 
revenue instead of reserves; enforcing existing codes relative to blighted properties, and limited 
staffing levels currently existing for addressing nuisance violations and abatements through the 
Code Enforcement office.  During discussion Ron Downey, City Treasurer, provided information 
on possible additional funding sources for the Fight the Blight program (i.e. Abandoned Building 
line item).  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor.  (President Rinne did not vote).  
Mr. Case moved to amend by removing $21,000.00 from reserves and placing it into the Animal 
Shelter line item, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Discussion included utilizing funding from recurring 
revenue instead of reserves, and Animal Shelter expenses and reduced hours in which it is open to 
the public.  During discussion and upon inquiry, Bob Fecht, Director, Cheyenne Animal Shelter, 
provided information on methods that had been reviewed to meet available budgetary funding vs. 
Shelter needs; decision to reduce hours, and plans to hire a second veterinarian.  Motion failed.  
Voting “no” – all members of the governing body present except President Rinne who did not 
vote.  Discussion followed on departmental supplemental budget requests noted in the working 
budget book, specifically Fire and Rescue Department requests; amount of funding that would be 
needed to include supplemental requests in the budget; inclusion of revenue in the budget from 
proposed sale of city property that has not yet been approved for sale; funding from reserves vs. 
recurring revenue, and employee raises and benefits.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Chief Greg 
Hoggatt, Fire and Rescue Department, provided information on supplemental requests for his 
department involving equipment replacements; plans to create a life cycle value for equipment; 
lease/option to purchase equipment scenario; department overtime and training; and response to 
calls through dispatch involving Fire and Rescue staff as well as ambulance response.  Mr. Shanor 
moved to amend by removing the approximately $771,818.00 proposed to be expended on merit 
(i.e. pay for performance) raises in the standard budget and removing the entire $6,534,851.00 
proposed in supplemental requests, seconded by Mr. Case.  Detective Aaron Willmarth, Police 
Protective Association, and Ms. Lowe commented in support of the value of City employees and 
pay for performance benefits.  Discussion included the pay for performance plan, which is still 
being developed by the administration, and implementation timeframe.  Mr. Downey and Eric 
Fountain, Mayor’s Chief of Staff, provided information in response to inquiries regarding the 
performance plan and status regarding implementation, with Mr. Downey noting that, if revenues 
are appropriated to do so, the budget could be supplemented at a later date to implement the plan.  

  
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Mr. 
Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Johnson and Mr. Roybal (President Rinne did not vote).  
Mr. Shanor moved to amend by adding the following expenditures into the standard budget 
request, seconded by Mr. Luna:  Add $16,268.00 in Facilities and Maintenance for what was 
identified as a FY2018 budgetary insufficiency; add in Police Admin proposed supplemental items 
of $65,156.00; add in Police Patrol proposed supplemental items of $292,471.00; Fire Admin add 
$45,126.00; Fire Training add $50,000.00; Fire Suppression add $125,000.00 for contractual wage 
adjustment and overtime; City Treasurer professional services add $30,000.00; to transfer an 
engineer position currently in the 5th penny budget to the City Engineer budget and add $33,000.00 
(other half in 5th penny funds); Planning and Development historic preservation grant matches add 
$6,000.00; economic support to Animal Shelter add $21,000.00, and add $50,000.00 for Fight the 
Blight efforts, for a total of approximately $741,021.00, and that the City Treasurer may make any 
adjustments needed due to any inaccuracies in the numbers enumerated in the amendment as well 
as if the expenditures do not add up to the proposed revenues the treasurer will reduce his sales tax 
estimate accordingly to match revenues to expenditures.  Discussion on the amendment included 
the following:  Verification of funding amount of $40,000.00 needed for the City Engineer 
position; areas included in funding for Police Admin and Police Patrol line items, including two 
additional police officer positions, and clarification of Patrol funding amount of $292,473.00 not 
$292,471.00; Fight the Blight program funding sources, and resources for nuisance enforcement.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey provided information on potential Community Development Block 
Grant funds that may be available for the Fight the Blight program.  Upon inquiry during 
discussion, Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, stated her concurrence with the proposed 
amendment regarding the Facilities and Maintenance budget but indicated an additional 
$10,000.00 is needed in the Public Works Admin budget for the City’s fleet vehicle pool costs.  
Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, Chief Hoggatt, and Mr. Downey also concurred with the 
proposed amendments relative to their respective budgets.  Mr. Downey clarified the general fund 
expenditure amount budgeted for merit based increases was $525,000.00 and the total of 
$771,818.00 for merit increases included other funds (i.e. 5th penny, 6th penny, sanitation and fleet, 
etc.).  Mr. Shanor moved to amend the amendment by adding $10,000.00 to the Public Works 
Admin budget and increasing the amount of $33,000.00 to $40,000.00 for the City Engineer 
position line item, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey advised adjustment would 
need to be made to the budget (due to previously approved amendment and the proposed 
amendments, if approved) to add an additional $525,000.00 in expenditures to the general fund.  
Motion to amend the amendment carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and President Rinne who did not vote.  
Motion to approve the amendment, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and President Rinne who 
did not vote.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Downey advised with the amendments the general fund budget 
was short by $246,525.00.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend as follows, seconded by Mr. Case:  Create 
a one-time revenue line item with an amount of $1,000,000.00 from reserves; create a one-time 
expenditure line item with $1,000,000.00 from reserves to be spent as follows:  $629,000.00 to 
Fire Suppression for self-contained breathing apparatus and other necessary equipment as deemed 
necessary by the Fire Chief; the remainder, approximately $371,000.00, to be spent at the Mayor’s 
discretion only on items identified in the proposed 2019 supplemental budget and of those items, 
only ones that are not payroll related; that the City’s total full-time employee count be limited to 

  
570 for FY2019, and for the Mayor to find the $246,525.00 in recurring revenues and recurring 
expenditure decreases to balance the general fund budget as well as to account for the other funds 
that were removed for merit raises to find another expenditure in those line items other than the 
merit raises.  Discussion included Fire Chief discretion and authorization to utilize the $629,000.00 
as he deemed appropriate.  Friendly amendment was offered by Mr. Roybal and accepted by Mr. 
Shanor and Mr. Case, as the maker and second to the motion to amend, to add “at his discretion” 
at the end of the phrase “as deemed necessary by the Fire Chief”.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” 
and President Rinne who did not vote. 
 
Following a 10-minute recess, President Rinne reconvened the meeting at 8:41 p.m.  
Motion to approve the budget ordinance as amended four times carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no” and 
President Rinne who did not vote. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 2.2.2 Planned Development Plan, Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, Section 5.1.3 
Official Zoning Map, and Section 5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from Laramie County Sweetgrass Planned 
Unit Development (PUD) to City of Cheyenne Sweetgrass PUD for 2,349.54 acres of land known 
as the Sweetgrass Land Area, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of 
College Drive and east of Avenue C).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on second reading with revised map, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of President Rinne 
who did not vote. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne for Fiscal Year 2018-2019 – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
sworn officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 2018-2019 – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Repealing in its entirety Chapter 12.16, Trees and 
Shrubs, and creating a new Chapter 12.16, Trees, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, 
of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming -- referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. 
Johnson and Mr. Laybourn) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Saddle 
Ridge, 13th Filing, a replat of a portion of Tract A, Saddle Ridge 12th Filing, a portion of Lot 1, 
Block 1, Cheyenne Hills Church Subdivision and a portion of the east half of Section 25, T.14N., 
R.66W., of the 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and including 
Pershing Boulevard, east of Farthing Road; also located south of Sage Road, east of Gunsmoke 
Road).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5928) 

  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for 
Woods Landing Estates, 2nd Filing, a replat of Tract 1, Woods Landing Estates, situated in the 
SE¼ of Section 14, T.14N., R.66W., of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located 
northwest of the intersection of Storey Boulevard and Whitney Road).  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#5929) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the City of Cheyenne’s desire and commitment to facilitating 
commercial passenger air service at the Cheyenne Regional Airport both now and in the future. 
Mr. Johnson declared a conflict of interest (business interest) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. 
White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. White moved to amend the “Now therefore 
be it resolved” clause by adding the words “safe and reliable” in the 3rd line following the word 
“support”, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Wendy Volk, Cheyenne Regional Air Focus Team (CRAFT), 
provided information on CRAFT; spoke in support of the resolution and commented on 
community benefits involving local passenger air service.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception of President  
Rinne who did not vote.  Discussion included:  Purpose of the resolution; new voter approved 
airport terminal currently under construction; local partnerships/stakeholders; facilitating air 
passenger service for economic development and community benefits, and widespread community 
collaborative efforts to provide commercial passenger air service.  It was noted that if the resolution 
was adopted, payment would not be allocated until discussions between all partnership and 
stakeholders were completed.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception of President Rinne who did 
not vote.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Johnson returned to Council Chambers. 
(#5930) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Department 
of Transportation for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Program Grant to provide the local 
match for operating funds for the City of Cheyenne for its Transit program, as authorized by 49 
U.S.C. § 5311.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5931) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for the 
Sweetgrass 1st Filing Subdivision situated in the N½ of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 
West, of the 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of College Drive and 
east of Avenue C) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the continuation of the Optional One Percent Sales Tax for 
calendar years 2019 through 2022 and pledging that all revenues derived from this tax will be used 
for projects described in this Resolution – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – To establish uniform procedures, fees, costs, and charges for inspecting, 
copying, and producing public records – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 

  
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution No. 5680 pertaining to adoption of a revised 
Cheyenne-Laramie County Board of Health fee schedule for various services and inspections – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving sanitary sewer service, by User’s Agreement, with Rocky 
Mountain REI, LLC, a county tract in SE¼, SW¼, Section 26, Township 14 North, Range 66 West 
of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
White) 
 
[CA] 
Standard Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Information Technology 
Division, and CenturyLink Sales Solutions, Inc., for Primary Rate Interface (PRI) 
telecommunications services for the City’s telephone system.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6842) 
 
[CA] 
Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Little America Hotels & Resorts, Inc., 
for placement of a billboard advertising sign on the Belvoir Ranch (revenue to the City).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6843) 
 
[CA] 
User Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation and Events 
Department, and Cheyenne Stampede Junior “A” Hockey, LLC, for hockey practices and games 
to be held at the City-owned Ice and Events Center (revenue to City).  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6844) 
 
[CA] 
Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and AM Signal, Littleton, Colorado, 
for traffic signal control software and affiliated hardware (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $247,158.56). (#6845) 
 
 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Asbestos and 
Technical Services, Inc., for services for the demolition and disposal of the residential structure 
located at 815 Capitol Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount 
not to exceed $43,000.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Eric Fountain, Mayor’s Chief of 
Staff, explained potential long-term community and neighborhood benefits of re-developing the 
property may off-set demolition costs.  Ron Downey, City Treasurer, concurred that the chance of 
recouping demolition costs would be minimal. Discussion included:  Public health and safety 
issues involving blighted properties within the community, and status of any liens upon the 
property.  Mr. Fountain stated the only known lien on the property was a tax lien.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson 
voting “no” and President Rinne who did not vote. (#6827) 
 
 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Noel Griffith Jr. & 
Associates Architects for analytical and design engineering for the construction of a new 
Municipal Court at 308 West 17th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming (1% Specific Purpose Sales Tax).  
Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, explained the importance of acquiring a professional 
consultant for the project and reviewed the project’s timeline of events resulting in the agreement 
being submitted for the agenda.  She provided information on the Phase I environmental site 
assessment; recommendation to have a structural engineer conduct an assessment, and the state 

  
asbestos program, and responded to inquiries regarding the project, condition of the building and 
contract services provided by TriHydro.  Discussion included:  Acquiring additional information 
prior to making a determination on the agreement; analysis and assessment of abatement costs; 
estimated overall project cost; services from a structural engineer and developing a scope of work 
relative to a structural assessment; asbestos abatement, and terms of the property purchase 
agreement relative to the closing timeframe.  During discussion, Ms. Nemecek and Amy Allen, 
City Engineer, provided information on structural engineer services vs. environmental engineer 
services, and coordination with the state asbestos program staff.  Noel Griffith, Noel Griffith Jr. & 
Associates Architects, explained the fees and services involved with the proposed agreement; 
stated there is a structural engineer affiliated with his firm who could conduct the structural 
assessment and help determine the asbestos remediation issue but they need a contract first to move 
forward.  He expressed his belief the entire project should be able to be completed within the 
amount of funding available and, upon inquiry, advised he would be able to provide an estimate 
for the financial cost of acquiring a structural engineer assessment and anticipated asbestos 
removal cost prior to the next City Council meeting.  Upon inquiry, Sylvia Hackl, City Attorney, 
advised the property purchase and sale agreement wasn’t fully executed by both parties until today, 
so timeframe requirements contained in the purchase agreement haven’t started yet, and she is 
working with the property owner’s agent on a closing schedule.  Mr. White reported there was no 
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve (in the 
amount of $657,500.00), seconded by Mr. Case. Mr. Shanor moved to postpone to June 11, 2018, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal. Comment followed in support of the postponement to allow time for Mr. 
Griffith to assist with a potential amendment to the agreement.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of President Rinne who did 
not vote. 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization, and Kimley Horn, Denver, Colorado, for the East Dell Range Boulevard / 
U.S. 30 Corridor Study - 2018 (MPO planning funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA]

Days, Inc., for the use of City-owned land and buildings for parking, camping, and other activities 
during the 2018 Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration -- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Frontier Days, Inc., for buses

Committee.

Memorandum of Understanding between Cheyenne Frontiers Days, Inc., the City of

County School District Number One to be used and operated for public transportation services

(2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.

Contract Modification No. 9 to Contract No. 6219 between the City of Cheyenne,

[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-10-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Public Works 
Department, and Modern Electric Co., Casper, Wyoming, for the 2018 Traffic Signal Upgrades 
Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid in an 
amount not to exceed $448,019.75). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-11-18 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering 
Department, and Simon Contractors, for the 2018 Chip Seal #1 Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).

[CA] 
Consideration of RFP-16-18 for a contract for furnishing and installing a parking access, 
permit and revenue control system – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne 
at the June 14, 2018 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Membership Business Meeting to be 
held in Pinedale, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (Councilman Dicky Shanor, 
Official Voting Delegate; Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, Alternate Voting Delegate). 
 
 
Appointments of John Brewster and Susan Brown to the Historic Preservation Board.  Mr. 
Shanor moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of President Rinne who did not vote.   
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, June 11, 2018 at 6:00 
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a restaurant liquor 
license application filed with the City Clerk’s office for Mancuso’s LLC d/b/a Esther’s, 2114 
Warren Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, June 11, 2018 at 6:00 
p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, for proposed budgets for the City of 
Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2019. 
 

  
 
Under other business:  
 
 
Mr. Johnson provided an update on the Depot Plaza splash pad fundraising efforts to date, 
and Mr. Case advised there will be a fundraiser at Danielmark’s Brewery on June 9, 2018 
benefitting the project. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 9:34 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Carol Intlekofer, MMC

LEASE AGREEMENT
THIS LEASE AGREEMENT IS MADE AND ENTERED INTO June 15, 2018, by and between JM Investments
LL.C., whose address is 4807 Connie Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82009, (hereinafter referred to as
“Landlord”], and Mancuso’s LLC, whose address is 4807 Connie Dr., Cheyenne, Wyoming 82009,
(hereinafter referred to as “Tenant”].
ARTICLE I - GRANT OF LEASE
Landlord, in consideration of the rents to be paid and the covenants and agreements to be performed and
observed by the Tenant, does hereby lease to the Tenant and the Tenant does hereby lease and take from
the Landlord the property and all improvements at 2114 Warren Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001.
ARTICLE II- LEASE TERM
Section I.
Total Term of Lease. The term of this lease shall begin on the 15th of June, 2018 through 1st
of November, 2022 continuing thereafter until terminated with thirty (30) day notice.
Section 2.
Rent. The tenant shall pay to Landlord the sum of $2,000 per month, payable on the first
day of the month and shall be considered advance payment for the month.
ARTICLE III - OTHER MATTERS
Landlord, JM Investments L.L.C., hereby grants permission to tenant, Manuso’s LLC to sell or sales of
liquor and malt beverages on the premises as granted under license by the State of Wyoming Liquor
Commission.

Dear Mayor and City Council:
Attached are the documents for your consideration in the application for a Restaurant
Liquor License for Mancuso’s, LLC (Mancuso’s at The Morris House). John & Maria Kopper
plan to reopen the Morris House to its glory days when the Bistro was at its peak. It’s a
Cheyenne staple that many people miss and still talk about to this day. Branding for the
house alone has already been accomplished and still kept vital through daily conversations
with locals or through Visit Cheyenne on trolley rides. The property is a turnkey operation
that just needs cleaning, a great menu and hardworking individuals like John and Maria and
the staff they have created at Bella Fuoco.
Mancuso’s restaurant will be a “white glove” dinner service only, Tuesday through
Saturday 5-9, typically with 2 major seating’s. The rotating menu will be a fusion of French,
Italian and Western American flavor. Patrons can experience amazing seafood to include a
raw bar with various fresh oysters, clams, mussels, shrimp and crab. A type of seafood
service that Cheyenne has never seen before. Top shelf beef, lamb, buffalo, veal, chicken
and pork will also be featured. Mancuso’s will have a wine list that will be second to none in
the State of Wyoming.
Please feel free to contact us at (307) 286-4188 at any time to discuss any questions or
concerns related to this application or to our proposed business. Thank you in advance for
your consideration.
çel
/
Joh
B
Maria E. Kopper
1
Owners, Mancuso’s, LLC

ORDINANCE NO. ___________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE PURSUANT TO SECTION 2.2.1 ZONING MAP
AMENDMENTS, SECTION 2.2.2 PLANNED DEVELOPMENT PLAN,
SECTION 5.1.2 ZONING DISTRICTS ESTABLISHED, SECTION 5.1.3
OFFICIAL ZONING MAP, AND SECTION 5.6.2. PUD – PLANNED UNIT
DEVELOPMENT DISTRICT, OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE,
CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM LARAMIE COUNTY
SWEETGRASS PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT (PUD) TO CITY OF
CHEYENNE SWEETGRASS PUD FOR 2,349.54 ACRES OF LAND KNOWN
AS THE SWEETGRASS LAND AREA, CITY OF CHEYENNE, LARAMIE
COUNTY, WYOMING (LOCATED SOUTH OF COLLEGE DRIVE AND
EAST OF AVENUE C).”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1.
That the owners of the property described herein have submitted a Sweetgrass
Planned Unit Development (“PUD”) Preliminary Plan pursuant to Section 5.6.2.f of the Cheyenne
Unified Development Code (“UDC”) which was conditionally-approved by the Governing Body on
October 10, 2016.
Section 2.
That on May 7, 2018, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted
a public hearing, reviewed the Applicant’s proposed Sweetgrass PUD Final Plan that is the subject
of this ordinance, found that it met the three conditions of approval of the PUD Preliminary Plan,
and recommended conditional-approval of said PUD Final Plan, rezoning the property described
herein to Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development-PUD.
Section 3.
That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the PUD Final
Plan that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4.
That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, Section
2.2.2, Planned Development Plan, Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, Section 5.1.3, Official
Zoning Map, and Section 5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District, of the UDC, the
application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property described in
Exhibit A to this ordinance is hereby changed to Sweetgrass Planned Unit Development (PUD).
Section 5.
That, in accordance with Section 5.6.2, the land use, design and development
standards and guidelines described in Exhibit B to this ordinance shall govern this PUD zoning
classification.
Section 6.
That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend
the Official Zoning Map as described in 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 8 below.
Section 7.
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs,
sentences, clauses, and phrases.
Section 8.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and
publication.

THIRD AND FINAL READING:___________________________________________________
__________________________________________
MARIAN J. ORR, MAYOR

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_91457129.pdf
Approved as to
rm only:
ORDINANCE NO.
_________
Date.
_______
ENTITLED:
“AN
ORDINANCE
DEFINING
THE
TERMS
AND
CONDITIONS
OF
EMPLOYMENT FOR NON-UNIFORMED EMPLOYEES OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE FOR FISCAL YEAR 2018-2019.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1. DEFINITIONS.
CITY EMPLOYEE/EMPLOYEES.
Employee or employees as used in this ordinance
means civilian City employees, but does not include the sworn personnel of the Cheyenne Police
Department below the rank of chief or the sworn personnel of the Cheyenne Fire Department
below the rank of chief.
Section 2. APPLICABILITY.
These terms and conditions of employment apply to all City employees.
Section 3. PURPOSE.
The purpose ofthis ordinance is to define the terms and conditions ofemployment for City
employees. In areas ofconflict between this ordinance and established federal, state or local laws,
the latter shall govern. This ordinance comprises the exclusive statement of City employee terms
and conditions ofemployment. My other agreement with or representation made to any employee
by a supervisor, agent or officer of the City of Cheyenne which is in conflict with or in addition to
this ordinance is specifically disclaimed by the City of Cheyenne, except for the City of Cheyenne
Personnel
Rules
and
Regulations
effective
February
17,
2015
(“Personnel
Rules
and
Regulations”), policies promulgated by the mayor pursuant to Chapter III, Section
1 of the
Personnel Rules and Regulations, and agreements entered into by and between supervisory
personnel and employees establishing specific job expectations, requirements or discipline which
are part ofthe employee’s official personnel file maintained by the Human Resources Department.
Section 4. COMPENSATION.
The compensation paid to City employees is set out in the pay plan schedule, which is
attached as Exhibit A and incorporated by reference herein. The compensation appropriate for
employees will be computed with reference to the grade of the individual employee.
The
governing body reserves the right, in its sole discretion, to modi& or change pay scales. Effective
July 1, 2008, City employees will receive, in addition to basic pay, longevity pay as set forth in
Exhibit B, attached hereto and incorporated by reference herein. Longevity raises will be effective
on the employee’s anniversary month. An individual who is recalled to City employment within
two (2) years after having been subjected to a reduction in force (RIF) will be paid longevity based
on total months of service, regardless of whether the months of service are consecutive.
Effective July 1, 2004, for any employee called to active military duty, the City of
Cheyenne will compensate the difference between the employee’s City base pay and military base
pay, provided the military base pay is lower than the city base pay. Base pay is defined in the
Personnel Rules and Regulations. This benefit is not available for employees hired after July 1,
2015.
Section 5. CONTINUATION OF EMPLOYMENT.
My employee covered by this ordinance who continues his/her tenn ofemployment during
and beyond the term of this ordinance shall be entitled to advancement in pay, if the mayor and
council approve ifinds to pay the increases.
1

Section 6. HEALTH. MEDICAL. DENTAL AND LIFE INSURANCE.
The City will negotiate with or seek bids from qualified health insurance providers and will
enter into a contract with a qualified carrier or carriers to provide City employees with a medical
insurance plan, which is on file in the Human Resources Department, for fiscal year 2017 2018
2018-2019. Each non-uniformed City employee eligible for health insurance coverage (including
members of the Governing Body) shall contribute clcvcn ten percent (14 10%) of the monthly
health insurance premium attributable to the level of coverage selected by that employee. This
payment will be a payroll deduction.
The City will maintain dental insurance on City employees pursuant to the dental plan on
file in the Human Resources Department.
The City will pay premiums for employees.
If an
employee wishes to maintain dental insurance under this plan for eligible dependents as defined
in Appendix B of the dental plan, he or she may do so by paying the premium for the additional
person(s) insured.
The City will pay premiums for term life insurance on all employees in accordance with
the term life insurance plan on file in the Human Resources Department and City Clerk’s Office.
Section 7. TERM.
The terms and conditions of employment set forth in this ordinance shall apply for fiscal
year 2017 2018 2018-2019 and shall be approved as part of the budget process. This ordinance
will terminate at the beginning of fiscal year 2018 2019 2019-2020.
SectionS. REPEAL OF OTHER ORDNANCES.
All other ordinances establishing specific terms and conditions of employment are
repealed.
Section 9. SEVERABILITY.
If any clause, sentence, paragraph or part of this ordinance or application to any person or
circumstance shall for any reason be adjudged by a court of competent jurisdiction to be invalid
such judgment shall not affect, impair or invalidate the remainder of this agreement.
Section 10. This ordinance shall be in MI force and effect upon passage and publication.

SECOND READING:__________________________
THIRD AND FINAL READING:__________________
Marian J. Orr, Mayor
(SEAL)

Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk
