# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_44368083.pdf
Approved as to
ORDNANCE NO._________
ENTITLED:
“AN
ORDNANCE
DEFINING
THE
TERMS
AND
CONDITIONS
OF
EMPLOYMENT FOR SWORN OFFICERS OF THE CHEYENNE POLICE
DEPARTMENT FOR FISCAL YEAR 2018-2019.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1. DEFINITIONS.
EMPLOYEE/EMPLOYEES
- Employee or employees as used in this ordinance means
sworn police officers, below the rank of chief.
Section 2. APPLICABILITY; AUTHORIZED POSITIONS.
The terms and conditions ofemployment as set forth herein shall apply to all employees as
defined herein. The Cheyenne Police Department shall consist of no more than one hundred six
(106) full-time employees as defined herein.
Section 3. PURPOSE.
The purpose of this ordinance is to define the terms and conditions of employment for
employees. The rights contained herein are in addition to any rights an employee may have under
the Cheyenne Police Department Policy Manual (as that document may be amended at any time,
before or after passage of this ordinance), and applicable federal, state and local laws. In areas of
conflict between this ordinance and established federal, state or local laws, the latter shall govern.
This ordinance comprises the exclusive statement of employee terms and conditions of
employment. Any other agreement with or representation made to any employee by a supervisor,
agent or officer of the City of Cheyenne which is in conflict with or in addition to this ordinance
is specifically disclaimed by the City of Cheyenne, except for the Cheyenne Police Department
Policy Manual and except for agreements entered into by and between supervisory personnel and
employees establishing specific job expectations, requirements or discipline which are part of the
employee’s official personnel file maintained by the Human Resources Department.
Section 4. CHEYENNE POLICE DEPARTMENT POLICY MANUAL AND BENEFIT
POLICY.
The Cheyenne Police Department Policy Manual including the benefit policy set forth in
Exhibit A, as they exist or are amended from time to time, are incorporated herein and made part
of these terms and conditions of employment and shall apply only to sworn police officers.
Section 5. COMPENSATION.
The compensation paid to employees is set forth in Exhibits B and C, which are attached
and incorporated by reference herein.
The compensation appropriate for the aforementioned
employees shall be computed with reference to the grade of the individual employee. Assignment
of employees to the appropriate grade under the new plan will be made on a case by case basis,
based upon employee hire date or promotion date, by the chief with the assistance of the director
of Human Resources. The governing body reserves the right, in its sole discretion, to modi& or
change pay scales.
Effective July 1, 2008, employees will receive, in addition to basic pay,
longevity pay as set forth in Exhibit D, attached hereto and incorporated by reference herein. An
individual who is recalled to City employment within two (2) years after having been subjected to
a reduction in force (RIP) will be paid longevity based on total months of service, regardless of
whether the months of service are consecutive. Effective July 1,2017, employees will receive, in
addition to basic pay, specialty on call pay as set forth in Exhibit E, attached hereto and
incorporated by reference herein.
I

Effective July 1, 2004, for any employee called to active military duty, the City of
Cheyenne will compensate the difference between the employee’s City base pay and military base
pay, provided the military base pay is lower than the City base pay. Base pay is defined as the
regular pay the employee would have received from the City had they not been called to active
military duty.
Section 6. CONTINUATION OF EMPLOYMENT.
Any employee covered by this ordinance who continues his or her term of employment
daring and beyond the term of this ordinance shall be entitled to advancement annually (subject to
the provisions of Section 5), as set forth in Exhibits B and C, upon recommendation of the
employee’s supervisor, if the mayor and council approve ifinds to pay the increases.
Section 7. HEALTH, MEDICAL. DENTAL AND LIFE INSURANCE.
The City will negotiate with or seek bids from qualified health insurance providers and will
enter into a contract with a qualified carrier or carriers to provide employees with a medical
insurance plan, which is on file in the Human Resources Department, for fiscal year 2017 2018
2018-2019. Each uniformed City employee eligible for health insurance coverage shall contribute
clcvcn ten percent (14 tO%) of the monthly health insurance premium attributable to the level of
coverage selected by that employee. This payment will be a payroll deduction.
The City will maintain dental insurance on employees pursuant to the dental plan on file in
the Human Resources Department. The City will pay premiums for employees. If an employee
wishes to maintain dental insurance under this plan for eligible dependents as defined in Appendix
B of the dental plan, he or she may do so by paying the premium for the additional person(s)
insured.
The City will pay premiums for term life insurance on all employees in accordance with
the term life insurance plan on file in the Human Resources Department and City Clerk’s Office.
Section 8. TERM.
The terms and conditions of employment set forth in this ordinance shall apply for fiscal
year 2017 2018 2018-2019 and shall be approved as part of the budget process. This ordinance
will terminate at the beginning of fiscal year 2018 2019 2019-2020.
Section 9. REPEAL OF OThER ORDINANCES.
All other ordinances establishing specific terms and conditions of employment are
repealed.
Section 10. SEVERABILITY.
If any clause, sentence, paragraph or part of this ordinance or application to any person or
circumstance shall for any reason be adjudged by a court of competent jurisdiction to be invalid
such judgment shall not affect, impair or invalidate the remainder of this agreement.
Section 11. This ordinance shall be in MI force and effect upon its passage and publication.
FIRST READING:_________
SECOND READING:_______
THIRD AND FINAL READING:___________________________________
Marian J. On, Mayor
(SEAL)
ATTEST:
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk
Published: Wyoming Tribune Eagle
2

EXHIBIT A
EMPLOYEE WAGE AND BENEFIT POLICY
THIS POLICY COVERS ALL COMMISSIONED OFFICERS OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
POLICE DEPARTMENT.
1.
MONTHLY WAGES.
a.
Effective July 1, 1997, all commissioned officers became covered by the City of Cheyenne
pay plan.
All step increases shall be on the officer’s original date of employment or promotion date.
All mmiversary raises will be given only after the officer has received an overall standard
performance evaluation.
If any employee is denied a step increase due to below standard
evaluation, that employee will be evaluated at three month intervals.
Whenever the evaluation
improves to an overall standard performance, the step increase will take effect pursuant to normal
step increase procedures, i.e., the first day of the month, received that month, after the first day
received the following month. Below standard evaluations require documentation that the officer
has been counseled in described areas of weakness.
b.
Lateral Transfer Program.
Lateral entry candidates must be currently certified as a Wyoming peace officer or in a
state with a reciprocity agreement with Wyoming. If not currently certified in Wyoming, lateral
entry candidates must take and pass the Wyoming POST challenge test, as well as the Wyoming
criminal law and domestic violence courses offered through the Wyoming Law Enforcement
Academy.
Subject to these requirements, the department offers a lateral transfer program for officers
with prior qualif3’ing law enforcement experience. All officers will be hired at a Grade 201
- Step
1. Officers completing a minimum ofsix months ofthe department’s eighteen month probationary
period may, at the chiefs discretion, be placed in the step program according to their experience
as follows:
2 to 4 years of experience:
Grade 201
- Step 3
4 to 6 years of experience:
Grade 201
- Step 4
6 + years of experience:
Grade 201
- Step 5
I

c.
Longevity Pay.
Effective July 1, 2004, eligible employees will be entitled to receive longevity pay based
on years of service. Longevity raises will be effective on the employee’s anniversary month.
d.
Snecialw On Call Pay.
Effective July 1, 2004, eligible employees will be entitled to receive specialty on call pay.
Employees are limited to receiving compensation for a maximum of two specialties per month.
An eligible employee is one who serves as a first line supervisor (field training officer) or
is required to be available for callout and does not receive any other compensation for being on
call.
2.
OVERTIME.
a.
Patrol Officers and Patrol Sergeants.
All sergeants and officers who are called in for overtime duties will receive a minimum of
two and one-halfhours ofovertime pay, excepting court appearances which are limited to one hour
overtime pay or compensatory time if the time spent is one hour or less. All court time in excess
of one hour is to be paid at time and one-half the classification rate. All patrol officers and patrol
sergeants shall be limited to eighty hours of accumulated compensatory time, at which time, they
must take paid overtime until they reduce below the eighty hour level.
Ml officers assigned acting sergeant duties will be compensated two hours overtime for
each shift. All sergeants assigned acting lieutenant duties will be compensated one hour straight
time for each shift.
Should the acting sergeant, lieutenant or captain duties extend beyond thirty consecutive
days, the department may temporarily promote the officer, and place the officer in the appropriate
pay grade of the new position.
3.
HOLIDAYS.
The officers and sergeants of the City of Cheyenne Police Department will be entitled to
those paid holidays that are set by Personnel Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne
effective February 17, 2015, and as enjoyed by personnel of other departments of the City.
The rate of pay for these holidays will be computed on the basis of 2,080 hours per year.
The officer working a holiday has the option of taking overtime pay or compensatory time at one
and one-half times the classification rate. If the holiday falls on an officer’s regular day off, he
will be given eight hours of straight pay or compensatory time. Likewise, if the holiday falls on
the officer’s vacation, or while on sick leave, the holiday will not be counted as vacation time or
sick leave.
2

4.
SICK LEAVE.
Each officer shall accumulate sick leave time at the rate of ten hours for each month of
continuous employment to a maximum of six hundred forty hours.
Officers who have comp’eted four years of continuous service and terminate from the City
shall receive 50% oftheir accrued sick leave at their regular base rate, not to exceed 240 hours. In
the case of retirement, an employee will be reimbursed filly for his or her accrued sick time up to
240 hours.
Officers who have not accumulated 480 hours at the beginning ofthe calendar year will be
entitled to twenty-four hours of goodie time each year, provided they use not more than forty hours
of sick leave during each calendar year. Officers who accumulate 480 hours at the beginning of
the calendar year will be entitled to forty hours goodie time each year, provided they use no more
than twenty-four hours of sick leave each calendar year; or thirty-two hours goodie time each year,
provided they use no more than thirty-two hours of sick leave each calendar year; or twenty-four
hours goodie time each year, provided they use no more than forty hours of sick leave each
calendar year. Goodie days under this section shall not be accumulated. Officers entitled to the
goodie time provided herein shall make application to the Chief of Police who shall schedule the
goodie time having regard for the convenience of the department. Upon termination, the twenty-
four, thirty-two or forty hours of goodie time are not paid as vacation.
For a period of one year following the date of an injury as defined in W.S. § 27-14-
1 02(a)(xi) sustained in the line of duty for the Cheyenne Police Department, an officer who is
totally disabled from performing any duties for the Department shall receive a monthly payment
from the City in an amount equal to the difference between: (1) the amount of any monthly benefit
received by the officer from any division of the Wyoming Department of Employment; and (2)
the average amount of monthly pay received by the officer during the twelve month period
preceding the date of injury, provided however, that if the officer has less then twelve months of
service prior to the injury, his or her average monthly pay shall be computed on the basis of actual
months of service. Upon the expiration of a period of one year following the date of an injury as
defined in W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xi), an officer who is totally disabled from performing any duties
for the Cheyenne Police Department shall be required to use accumulated sick leave.
5.
SICK BANK.
The Sick Bank is to be used by officers in the case of serious illness or injury which is not
covered under Worker’s Compensation, and after they have exhausted their accumulated sick
leave.
The Sick Bank is to be established on the effective date of this policy with a balance of
1,200 hours contributed by the City. The Sick Bank will maintain a balance limited to 1,200 hours.
This balance to be renewed by the City, if any days are used, on January 1 of each year.
3

A contributory pooi (Sick Pool) of 4,160 maximum sick hours will be established on the
effective date ofthis policy. Sworn officers with excess sick leave (over 640 hours) may contribute
their excess sick leave to the Sick Pool January l of each year.
The Sick Bank and Sick Pool are to be administered by the Sick Bank Board.
This Board
will consist of the Chief of Police, the City Human Resources Director and the President of the
Police Protective Association or his designee.
Policies and procedures for administering the Sick Bank and Sick Pool are to be made by
the Sick Bank Board, including renewing depleted balances. These policies and procedures are to
be in writing and available, upon request, to all sworn officers. Request for Sick Bank/Sick Pool
hours are to be made in writing to the Sick Bank Board, in accordance with their policies and
procedures. All requests for Sick Bank/Sick Pool must be approved by the Board prior to use of
those days.
6.
VACATION.
Each officer shall be entitled to a vacation of eighty-eight hours after one year of
continuous service, and shall be entitled to an additional eight hours of vacation for each year of
uninterrupted service provided that an officer is entitled to earn no more than 216 hours ofvacation
in any one year. Insofar as possible, vacation time shall be determined by division and squad, with
seniority rank having preference within each division and squad.
Not more than 240 hours vacation accrual can be carried from one calendar year to the next
calendar year. All officers with 240 hours vacation accrual at the beginning of a calendar year are
required to use 120 hours of vacation each calendar year.
Accumulated vacation between 240
hours and 336 hours on December31 of each year shall be reimbursed with the December payroll.
All vacation time over 336 hours shall be deemed waived.
On separation from service, an officer shall be paid for all accumulated vacation days
earned and not taken, including the year of separation, proportionate to the time employed in that
year.
7.
PERSONAL DAY.
Each MI-time uniformed police officer with one year of continuous service will be
awarded one personal day each fiscal year. A personal day shall be defined as eight hours.
This personal day may be used by the employee at his or her discretion, with supervisory
approval. Personal days must be used in the year in which they are awarded. Personal days cannot
be used to extend a holiday or be broken into increments of less than a normal working day. They
do not carry over from one year to the next.
4

8.
REDUCTION IN FORCE.
Officers laid off in accordance with the provisions of this section will be eligible for rehire
or reinstatement into a position of the same classification in the inverse order of layoff. If rehired
or reinstated, however, no officer shall accumulate time in service during the period of lay off. An
officer laid off under these provisions shall be protected for a period of twenty-four months from
time of separation, pursuant to state statute. The department shall notif’ protected officers of any
vacancy as soon as known to the department. The officer shall noti& the department with-in five
days of his or her receipt of notice of his or her intent and must report at least fifteen days after
receipt of notice.
9.
CLOTHING AND EQUIPMENT ALLOWANCES.
a.
Clothing Allowance.
Detective personnel and others, as defined by the chief, shall be entitled to $60.00 per
month clothing allowance.
b.
The department will provide all employees with the following equipment:
i.
Uniforms;
ii.
Ballistic vest;
iii.
Leather gear;
iv.
Handcuffs;
v.
Baton;
vi.
Duty t-shirts;
vii.
Handgun; and
viii.
Duty footwear.
10.
MEDICAL AND LIFE INSURANCE.
All eligible commissioned officers may elect to participate in any of the health insurance
plans offered by insurance companies contracted by the City to provide medical and life insurance.
11.
EXTENSION OF MEDICAL COVERAGE.
a.
All retirees may elect to participate in any of the health insurance plans offered by
the City’s health insurance provider. Premiums will be paid by retirees.
5

b.
If an officer sustains an injury as defined in W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xi) in the line of
duty for the Cheyenne Police Department and is awarded permanent total disability benefits as
defined in W.S. § 27-1 4-102(a)(xvi), or if an officer dies as a result of an injury as defined in W.S.
§ 27-14-102(a)(xi) sustained in the line of duty, the officer and his or her eligible dependents shall
be offered health insurance at the expense ofthe City subject to the following terms and conditions:
i.
The City’s obligation to provide health insurance coverage for the officer
shall terminate when the officer becomes eligible for coverage under Medicare or
Medicaid;
ii.
The City’s obligation to provide health insurance for the officer’s spouse
shall terminate when the spouse re-marries, becomes eligible for health insurance coverage
under any employer-sponsored health insurance program, or becomes eligible for Medicare
or Medicaid, whichever occurs first; and
iii.
The City’s obligation to provide health insurance coverage for an eligible
dependent of the officer shall terminate when that dependent reaches the age of twenty-six
years, or becomes eligible for Medicare or Medicaid, whichever first occurs.
c.
For the purposes of this paragraph ( II), the word “spouse” shall refer only to the
person to whom the officer is legally married at the time the officer is awarded permanent total
disability benefits as defined by W.S. § 27-l4-102(a)(xvi) and the phrase “eligible dependent”
shall refer only to a child of the officer, including any child adopted by the officer before the child
reached the age of fourteen years. Step-children, foster children, and wards of the officer under
any guardianship or conservatorship may be eligible dependents for coverage under the plan
documents for health insurance coverage provided by the City.
In the case of a quali1’ing event, the eligible person may change his or her election within
thirty days of said qualiing event or wait until open enrollment.
The City of Cheyenne may from time to time require verification of permanent disability
of member, of full time student status or mental or physical disability.
12.
PPA BUSINESS.
With prior inmwdiate supervisor and division head approval, time off may be given for
conducting PPA business including lobbying at the state legislature.
13.
DUES FOR MEMBERSHIP.
The dues for membership in the PPA shall be set forth by the PPA by-laws.
The City
agrees to deduct this sum, as a payroll deduction, from such member’s monthly paycheck and
remit these dues to the PPA.
6

14.
NEW LANGUAGE.
All proposed changes to the wage and benefit policy will be distributed in writing a
minimum of thirty days prior to the effective date. The staff officers of the department will be
available to discuss and explain and take any comments relative to the proposed change. All other
policies will be distributed in writing and once each year updated in this manual.
7

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_88312741.pdf
App
forr
ORDINANCE NO.
Datu /23i if
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE REPEALING IN ITS ENTIRETY CHAPTER 12.16,
TREES AND SHRUBS, AND CREATING A NEW CHAPTER 12.16, TREES,
OF TITLE 12, STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND PUBLIC PLACES, OF THE
CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”
BE IT HEREBY ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
Section 1. That Chapter 12.16, Trees and Shrubs, ofTitle 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public
Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby repealed in its entirety.
Section 2. That Chapter 12.16, Trees, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of
the Code ofthe City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created as follows:
CHAPTER 12.16
TREES
12.16.010
Purpose—Authority--General provisions.
A.
The city recognizes the substantial economic, environmental, aesthetic and public
health benefits oftrees within the community. It is the policy ofthe city to protect and enhance the
community’s urban forest. The purpose of this chapter is to promote and protect the public safety
and general welfare by providing regulation, and to establish policies regarding the planting,
maintaining and removal of frees through recommended horticultural and arboricultural practices,
and to establish and enforce professional standards for city business licensees, pursuant to licensing
provisions of Chapters 5.04 and 5.80 of the city code, pertaining to operating a tree maintenance
business.
B.
Authority is granted to cities and towns by Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-1OI(a)(xiii) and (xli)
to license and regulate business activities within the city for the health, safety and welfare of its
citizens.
C.
The requirements of this chapter will not be deemed to impose any liability for
damages or duty of care and maintenance upon the city or any of its officers, agents or employees.
12.16.020
City forester—Urban Forestry guidelines.
A.
To promote the above purpose, the city shall employ a person known as the city
forester who shall have the power to establish and enforce rules, regulations, standards, and
specifications for the protection, planting, pruning, treatment, removal or any maintenance oftrees
located upon the right-of-way of any street, alley, sidewalk or other city owned property in the city
and concerning the peffomiance of such work on public or private property within the city by
licensees under Chapters 5.04 and 5.80. These guidelines shall be titled “City of Cheyenne Urban
Forestry guidelines,” shall be in writing, and shall not be effective until signed by the mayor. These
guidelines, shall be on file and open for inspection by the public with the forestry division and office
of the city clerk.
B.
The city forester or designee is authorized to assess basic costs and fees, including
an administrative fee to cover expenses related to personnel and equipment relative to enforcement
of the provisions of this chapter, and as set out in the Urban Forestry guidelines.
1

12.16.030
Definitions.
The following definitions shall apply in the interpretation and enforcement of this chapter:
“City forester” shall mean the manager or director of the Urban Forestry division or
designee.
“City property” shall mean and include all real property owned by and leased to the city of
Cheyenne, not including the public right-of-way.
“Downtown” means the boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority
District as described in Section 2.76.040 of the city code.
“Maintenance” shall mean any and all work performed on trees including, but not limited
to, planting, watering, pruning, removing, stump grinding, treating for insects or diseases, protecting
and enhancing soils, and preserving and protecting trees during construction.
“Noxious tree” means a tree that is designated a “designated noxious weed” by the Wyoming
Weed and Pest Control Act, Wyoming Statute § 11-5-101, et seq.
“Nuisance tree” shall mean a tree with undesirable characteristics as defined in the Urban
Forestry guidelines and declared to be a public nuisance by the city forester or designee.
“Private property” means all land and improvements, including fixtures and appurtenances,
located within the property lines of a property.
“Property owner” shall mean any person or entity having a legal or equitable interest in real
property and its fixtures and appurtenances, including the interests of a tenant or lessee.
“Public right-of-way” shall mean the entire area between the curb, or street where no curb
exists, and the property line of a property and between the centerline of the alley and property line
of a property.
“Responsible party” shall mean the property owner or an entity or person who, acting as an
agent for or in any other legal capacity on behalf of the owner, has authority over property subject
to this chapter or who is responsible for the maintenance or management of said property.
“Tree” or “trees” shall mean any perennial woody plant, usually, but not necessarily, single
stem and long-lived with a height greater than overall spread.
12.16.040
Responsibility for trees in public right-of-way.
A.
Public right-of-way. The responsible party ofproperty abutting the public right-of-
way shall have the duty to maintain trees on the abutting portions ofthe public right-of-way.
B.
Downtown.
The city recognizes the significance of its downtown relative to the
community’s economy, culture and history, and supports trees and landscaping features to enhance
the vitality of downtown. The city shall be responsible for the planting, care, maintenance, and
removal oftrees in the public right-of-way within the designated Downtown Development Authority
Special District.
C.
Written Agreements.
A written agreement may establish responsibility for trees
located on public rights-of-way as the responsibility of the city or another entity such as a
homeowner’s association or special district. This written agreement shall be approved by the city
forester and the city attorney’s office and filed with the city clerk.
2

12.16.050
Obstruction of the public right-of-way.
A.
The city is responsible for clearing trees away from traffic signs and traffic control
devices to maintain clear visibility.
B.
The responsible party for any property located within the city shall:
1.
Maintain all trees to provide clearance over streets and alleys of at least
fourteen (14) feet and over sidewalks of at least eight (8) feet.
2.
Maintain an unobstructed site distance triangle pursuant to the Urban forestry
guidelines.
3.
Remove all stumps in the public right-of-way to at least eight (8) inches
below original grade and replace with top soil.
C.
When the city forester or designee determines a tree is in such a condition that it
endangers the safe public use of streets, alleys, and sidewalks, the city forester shall issue a notice
ofviolation requiring corrective steps in accordance with Section 12.16.112.
D.
In the case of an emergency, the city forester or designee may cause the immediate
necessary remedial action in accordance with Section 12.16.110 and Section 12.16.112.
12.16.060
Permit required for planting, pruning or removal of trees in public places—
Application—Inspection.
A.
It shall be unlawful for any person to plant, cut, trim, prune, remove, destroy or cause
to be planted, cut, trimmed, pruned, removed or destroyed any tree within the public right-of-way
of any street or sidewalk, or upon other city-owned property within the city without having first
obtained a written permit therefor. For purposes ofthis section, “within the public right-of-way of
any street or sidewalk, or upon other city-owned property” shall not include trees located on private
property that encroach upon or overhang public property. Owners of properties adjoining alley
rights-of-way may cut, trim, prune or remove trees located within such rights-of-way without a
permit.
B.
Any person desiring to plant, cut, trim, prune, remove or destroy any tree within the
public right-of-way of any street or sidewalk, or upon other city-owned property within the city,
must first file a written application for a permit at the office of the city forester, if a permit is
required under this section. This application is available from the city forester and it must set forth
the name and address ofthe applicant, the name and address ofthe person doing the work, the kind
or species oftree to be planted, cut, trimmed, pruned, removed or destroyed and the location ofthe
same, together with such other information as the city forester shall require.
C.
The applicant must, at the time of making the application, agree in writing, in all
respects, to hold the city harmless and protect the city and the public at all times in connection with
the planting, cutting, trimming, pruning, removal or destruction of any tree to be planted, cut,
trimmed, pruned, removed or destroyed under such permit.
D.
The city forester shall inspect the location of the tree to be planted, cut, trimmed,
pruned, removed or destroyed, and if, in his or her opinion, it is desirable that such tree be planted,
cut, trimmed, pruned, removed or destroyed, as the case may be, the city forester shall issue a
permit. Such permit shall set forth the name ofthe person who shall perform the work, the location
at which the tree is or shall be located and any conditions or requirements made by the city forester
in connection therewith.
3

12.16.070
Nuisance and noxious trees.
A.
Nuisance trees.
The city forester or designee may declare certain trees to be a
nuisance tree as described in the Urban Forestry guidelines.
B.
Noxious trees. Ifthe city forester or designee determines a tree is a noxious tree, it
shall be controlled pursuant to state law.
12.16.080
Infected or infested trees.
A.
The city forester or designee may inspect any tree or tree part, including logs or
branches existing or growing upon any property located within the city.
The city forester or
designee shall conduct surveys to determine if any destructive or communicable disease or other
pestilence exists which may be detrimental to or endanger the good health and well being oftrees
in the city.
B.
Upon discovery of any infected or infested tree, the city forester shall noti& the
responsible party in writing, subject to Section 12.16.112, of the infected or infested tree and order
such responsible party to eradicate, remove, or otherwise control the condition within the time
specified in the written notice.
C.
Failure to comply with a notice regarding an infected or infested tree will be followed
pursuant to Section 12.16.112.
12.16.090
Tree protection, damages and cost recovery.
A.
It shall be unlawfiil to injure, damage, destroy or remove any tree situated upon city
property.
B.
No person shall attach any material to a tree on city property.
C.
Trees on city property shall be protected during construction projects as outlined in
the Urban Forestry guidelines.
12.16.110
Right of entry.
A.
In order to accomplish the purposes of this chapter, the city forester or designee, or
any contractor hired by the city forester, shall have the right to enter public or private property
within the city in order to inspect trees and other vegetation upon written notice conspicuously
posted upon the affected property and also deposited in the United States mail, addressed to the
owner of record at the address on the assessment roll of the Laramie County Assessor. The notice
shall set forth a date certain for inspection and entry.
B.
Subject to the notice requirements of Section 12.16.112, the city forester or a
contractor hired by the city forester is also authorized to go upon any public or private property in
the city for the purpose of cutting, trimming, pruning and removing trees that the city forester has
determined present a danger to persons using, or property located upon, the public rights-of-way or
other city-owned property within the city, or in order to treat or eliminate a destructive or
communicable disease or insect infestation.
C.
In the case of an emergency or where the city forester has determined an immediate
threat to public safety, the city forester shall have the authority to take any action or abatement
necessary to eliminate the threat to public safety.
4

12.16.111
Interference.
No person shall prevent, delay or interfere with the city in regards to the enforcement ofthis
chapter or activities required in conducting the urban forestry program; provided, however, nothing
contained in this chapter shall be construed as an attempt to prohibit any legal or equitable remedy
in any court ofcompetent jurisdiction for the protection of property rights by the owner ofproperty
within the city.
12.16.112
Notice.
A.
Notice of violation. The city forester or designee shall give written notice to the
responsible party of any property to correct any condition in order to comply with the terms of this
chapter. The notice shall describe the property affected and set forth the action to be taken by the
responsible party.
The notice shall also prescribe a time for compliance.
The notice shall be
conspicuously posted upon the affected property and also deposited in the United States mail,
addressed to the owner of record at the address on the assessment roll of the Laramie County
Assessor.
B.
Failure to comply.
Should the responsible party of any property refuse or fail to
comply with the terms ofa notice during the time prescribed, the city or its designee may enter upon
the premises and remove, treat or otherwise care for trees and vegetation in order to correct the
existing condition.
In the event that the city is required to perform any corrective work on the
premises, the costs thereof shall be assessed against the responsible party.
C.
Abatement and costs. If the city abates a violation on private property or on the
public right-of-way, the city forester or designee will noti1’ the responsible party of the cost of
abatement by United States mail addressed to the owner of record at the address on the assessment
roll of the Laramie County Assessor. The costs of abatement shall include all direct and indirect
costs abatement including, but not limited to, manpower, equipment, expenses and administrative
costs incurred by the city. Such costs shall be due and owing from the property owner to the city.
Such costs, expenses and fees may be collected in any manner prescribed by law.
12.16.113
Violation—Penalty.
Any person who violates the provisions of this chapter will be deemed guilty of a
misdemeanor, punishable pursuant to the general penalty provision of the city code.
Section 3.
That this ordinance shall be in fill force and effect upon its approval and
publication.
FIRST READING:
_____________
SECOND READING:
____________
THIRD AND FINAL READING:
____________
Marian J. On, Mayor
(SEAL)
AHEST:
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk
Published:
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
5

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_85410188.pdf
 
ORDINANCE NO.   
  
 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING VARIOUS CHAPTERS AND SECTIONS 
CONTAINED 
WITHIN 
TITLE 
5, 
BUSINESS 
LICENSES 
AND 
REGULATIONS, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, PERTAINING TO NOTARIZATION OF APPLICANT 
SIGNATURES.” 
 
BE IT HEREBY ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That various chapters and sections contained within Title 5, Business Licenses and 
Regulations, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby amended as set out in this 
ordinance.  Chapters and sections currently existing within Title 5, Business Licenses and 
Regulations, not set out or clarified by an [Editing note: …] within this ordinance remain 
unchanged. 
 
5.52.050 Application for massage therapist permit. 
 
 
A. Initial Application.  Initial application for a massage therapist permit must be made to the 
city clerk in the same manner as provided for a massage business establishment license, 
accompanied by the annual nonrefundable massage therapist permit fee of one hundred fifteen 
dollars ($115.00).  The application must contain, but may not be limited to, the following 
information: 
 
 
14. Written declaration by the applicant, under penalty of perjury, that the information 
contained within the application is true and correct, such declaration being signed and dated before 
a licensed notary public or person authorized by law to administer oaths; 
 
5.72.030 Business license required. 
 
 
B. An application for a public transportation business license must contain such information 
and documentation as may be determined by the city clerk and the chief of police including, but 
not limited to the following: 
 
 
8. Affirmation that the information contained and submitted with the application form is 
complete, true and correct.  Each applicant shall sign an authorization, in a form prescribed by the 
chief of police, that shall be sufficient to authorize the chief of police to conduct criminal and other 
background investigations in the manner provided by Section 5.72.010 (C) of the city code.  Each 
application shall be signed by the applicant in the present of a licensed notary public.  Each AND 
THE applicant shall verify, that the statements contained in the application are true and correct. 
The chief of police may recommend the denial of any application that contains false, misleading 
or incomplete information. 
 
 

5.72.050 Public transportation driver’s permit required. 
 
 
B. An application for a public transportation driver’s permit must contain the following 
information, and such other information and supporting documentation as requested by the chief 
of police: 
 
 
7. Affirmation that the information contained and submitted with the application form is 
complete, true and correct.  Each applicant shall sign an authorization, in a form prescribed by the 
chief of police, that shall be sufficient to authorize the chief of police to conduct criminal and other 
background investigations in the manner provided by Section 5.72.010 (C) of the city code.  Each 
application shall be signed by the applicant in the presence of a licensed notary public.  Each AND 
THE applicant shall verify that the statements contained in the application are true and correct.  
The chief of police may recommend the disapproval of any application that contains false, 
misleading, or incomplete information. 
 
5.76.030 Temporary or transient merchant – Comprehensive –  
 
 
 
License required – Application. 
 
 
B. Persons desiring a temporary or transient merchant business license, including an annual 
comprehensive temporary or transient merchant license, must submit written application on forms 
provided by the city clerk, together with payment of the licensing fee, to the office of city clerk.  
Each application shall be signed by the applicant in front of a notary public and notarized by same. 
 
[Editing note:  Remainder of existing city code sub-section 5.76.030 (B) is unchanged.]  
 
5.76.040 Residential door-to-door solicitors – License and 
 
 
 
License identification badge required – Application. 
 
 
B. Persons desiring a residential door-to-door solicitors business license must submit a written 
application on forms provided by the city clerk, together with payment of the business license fee 
and license identification badge fee, to the office city clerk.  Each application shall be signed by 
the applicant in front of a notary public and notarized by same. 
 
[Editing note:  Remainder of existing city code sub-section 5.76.040 (B) is unchanged.]  
 
5.78.030 Business license required. 
 
 
B. Any person desiring to conduct, maintain, or operate a towing/recovery operation within 
the city must first obtain a business license by submitting to the city clerk an application, together 
with a non-refundable annual license fee, on forms provided by the city clerk. 
 
[Editing note:  Remainder of existing city code sub-section 5.78.030 (B) is unchanged with the 
exception of sub-section (B) (6) below.]  
 
 

 
6. Affirmation that the information contained in and submitted with the application form is 
complete, true and correct.  Each application must be signed by the applicant in the presence of a 
licensed notary public.  
 
Section 2.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_29385461.pdf
 
ORDINANCE NO.   
  
 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 5.12.140, PERSONS UNDER 
THE AGE OF TWENTY-ONE (21) YEARS – RESTRICTIONS – 
PROVISIONS, OF CHAPTER 5.12, ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR AND MALT 
BEVERAGES, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING.” 
 
BE IT HEREBY ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That Section 5.12.140, Persons under the age of twenty-one (21) years – Restrictions – 
Provisions, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, within Title 5, Business 
Licenses and Regulations, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as 
set out in this ordinance.  Sections currently existing within Chapter 5.12 not set out or clarified 
by an [Editing note: …] within this ordinance remain unchanged. 
 
5.12.140 
Persons under the age of twenty-one (21) years – Restrictions – Provisions. 
 
 
A. Except as provided in this section, no RETAIL LIQUOR licensee, or permittee, 
employee, server or agent thereof shall knowingly permit any person under the age of twenty-one 
(21) years to enter or remain in a licensed building: 
 
 
1. 
Where alcoholic liquor and malt beverages being sold, dispensed and consumed are 
the primary source of revenue for the licensee’s or permittee’s business operation unless the 
licensee or permittee also provides a full-service restaurant, as defined in Wyo. Stat. Section 12-
1-101(a)(xiv), operation within the building, and the restaurant is consistently open to the public; 
 
 
a. 
If a licensed building contains a separate room designated for the sale of alcoholic 
liquor and malt beverages for off-premise consumption, commonly referred to as a package store, 
an underage person may temporarily be allowed within the room but must be accompanied by his 
or her parent, spouse or legal guardian who is at least twenty-one (21) years of age. 
 
 
2. 
Where alcoholic liquor and malt beverages are sold, dispensed and/or consumed in a 
business establishment that provides adult entertainment, exotic dancing or similar venues; 
  
 
3. 
Unless the person is a license or permit holder, pursuant to provisions of Wyo. Stat. 
Section 12-1-101, et seq., and city code, or 
 
 
4. 
Unless the primary purpose of the business operation within the licensed building is 
operation as a bowling center business and commercially provided food services. 
 
[Editing note:  Remainder of existing city code sub-section 5.12.140 (A) is unchanged.]  
 

Section 2.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

1 
STAFF REPORT - SUMMATION 
  
Simpson Estates 
Zone District Map Amendment Process

UDC-18-00146-01

Seth Lloyd

REQUIRED DOCUMENTS SUBMISSION: The following required documents have been submitted and 
reviewed by staff: 
• 
Master Application; Zone Change Map; List of neighbors to be notified; Summary of why 
proposal is being sought; Draft Neighbor Notification Letter  
• 
Neighbor Notification Letter – received 5/10/2018 (sent to fulfill notification requirements) 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION RECOMMENDATION:  At their regular meeting on June 4, 2018, the City 
of Cheyenne Planning Commission recommended approval of the zone change request from MUB Mixed-
use Business Emphasis to CB Community Business by a vote of 5-0. 
 
RECOMMENDATION:  Staff recommends approval of the zone change request from MUB Mixed-use 
Business Emphasis to CB Community Business.

WORK DESCRIPTIONS: A zone change request from MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for Simpson Estates 
  
LOCATION: 
1. Legal Description: Lots 1-2, Block 1, Simpson Estates. 
2. Location: west of and adjacent to College Drive, north of Pershing Boulevard.

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00146-01 
3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This zone change application proposes changing a land from MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business. This land totals about 1.03 acres. This land is currently vacant, and the applicant 
proposes developing the property with an automobile service use (specifically, a car wash).  
 
The Comprehensive Plan and the Unified Development Code support regional-serving commercial uses at 
this site. PlanCheyenne specifically encourages MUB and CB zoning for the activity center located at 
College and Pershing. MUB is the current zone and CB is the proposed zone. 
 
MUB zoning is intended for areas with small blocks, a high degree of pedestrian amenities, and buildings 
built near the street. CB zoning is intended for regional-serving commercial areas, automobile-oriented 
development, and buildings setback from the street. The blocks in this area are larger and the current 
commercial development in this area (between Cheyenne Street and Pershing) match the development 
patterns anticipated by areas zoned CB. By zoning this parcel CB, the development on this site will better 
match the existing development in the vicinity. The proposed CB zone is more suitable to this site than 
the existing MUB zoning. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of Section 2.2.1.d of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code, staff recommends approval of this zone change. 
 
 

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00146-01 
4 
 
LOCATION/VICINITY MAP:  
 
 
Map # 1 – Project Location/ Vicinity Map

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00146-01 
6 
 
ZONE CHANGE DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions 
on a proposed Zone Change Request, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following criteria of Section 2.2.1.d: 
 
1. PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the 
development policies and physical patterns identified in 
the Comprehensive Plan.  
 
PlanCheyenne is supportive of this zone change. 
 
Principle 1.4 is supportive of this zone change, as it 
encourages revitalization and infill development which 
maximizes existing infrastructure. Policies within 
PlanCheyenne encourage concentrating commercial uses 
in activity centers, this property is in the Pershing/College 
activity center and will keep commercial development 
close to a major intersection. 
 
The future land use map designates this area as Mixed-
use Commercial Emphasis. This mixed-use future land 
use designation is intended to develop with primarily 
commercial uses with secondary residential uses, with the 
commercial uses ideally servicing the community. The current MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis 
zone is the suggested zone for this future land use designation. This area is also designated as a 
Mixed-use Commercial Activity Center. Mixed-use Commercial Activity Centers are intended to 
develop with a mix of commercial uses with some housing. The CB and MUB zones are both 
considered appropriate zones for Mixed-use Commercial Activity Centers. 
 
With the intersection of College and Pershing anticipated to develop with commercial uses which 
serve a larger area, the CB zone is an appropriate zone for this area. 
 
 
2. SURROUNDING AREA - The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering 
existing zoning, the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the 
guidance of that plan for these areas.  
 
The proposed zoning designation is compatible with nearby properties as it matches some of the 
commercial zoning in the area and as nearby blocks include CB commercial-style development in the 
MUB zone. Under modern standards, the MUB zone is intended to develop with commercial buildings 
near the street and parking to the side or rear. CB zoning anticipates development with parking and 
vehicle activities in the front with the building in the rear. The commercially developed properties to 
the east, west, and south all match the anticipated development patterns of the current CB zone 
better than the anticipated development patterns of the current MUB zone. While nearby properties 
are zoned MUB, the development of this site in a CB style would be compatible with the surrounding 
area. 
Map #3 – Future Land Use  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00146-01 
7 
 
The perimeter of this zone change proposal is approximately 1100 linear feet, of this total 
approximately:  
• 
67.1% is contiguous to area currently zoned MUB (south, west, north – across Cheyenne 
Street) 
• 
31.1% is contiguous to area currently zoned CB (east – across College Drive) 
• 
1.9% is contiguous to area currently zoned MR (northwest – across Cheyenne Street) 
 
 
3. ZONING: BASIC STANDARDS & USES - The suitability of the 
subject property for development according to the standards 
and uses of the existing zoning district, compared to the 
suitability of the subject property for development according 
to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district.  
 
The property is able to develop under the standards and 
uses of the MUB zone district. The MUB zone allows a 
variety of uses, but automobile-centered businesses in 
the MUB zone require a conditional use approval. MUB 
zoning is intended for areas with smaller blocks and a 
pedestrian-scale environment. Buildings in the MUB zone 
are intended to be placed closer to the street. 
 
The property is also able to develop under the standards 
and uses of the CB zone district. CB zone is intended for 
regional, auto-oriented commercial development with 
buildings setback from the street. Most automobile-
centered businesses are a use-by-right in the CB zone district. CB zoned areas can have larger 
blocks. 
 
While the area around this site has a well-connected street network, the block sizes are large and 
much of the commercial development south of Cheyenne Street reflects a CB-zone style of 
development. This property appears to more suitable to develop under the proposed CB zone than 
the current MUB zone. 
 
 
4. SUBDIVISION STANDARDS - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
according to the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning.  
 
The applicant is proposing this zone change without proposing any changes to the streets or blocks. 
This neighborhood has an established street network. The development is along College Drive, and 
access to this property may be limited by WYDOT. This zone change does not negatively affect the 
ability of this land and the surrounding neighborhood to meet any of the other standards found in 
Article 4. 
 
 
Map #4 – Zoning Districts  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00146-01 
8 
 
5. ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY, & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district, and achieve the Design Objectives of 
any specific standards. 
 
The CB zoning district is intended to develop with moderate to large scale businesses and to serve 
multiple neighborhoods. The properties in this area of the community allow for moderate-scale 
commercial operations which could service multiple neighborhoods. A minor landscape buffer would 
be required between redevelopment on this site and the adjoining MUB zoned parcels. This land is 
able to meet the design objectives of the CB zone.  
 
 
6. ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval.  
 
The applicant is proposing rezoning this parcel with the intent to use a vacant parcel for commercial 
use. Any commercial development on this site would be able to mirror the existing commercial 
development patterns in the area. While this property could easily develop with a commercial use 
meeting CB zoning standards, other specific development proposals may prefer MUB-style 
development patterns. By changing from MUB to CB, this property would only be able to develop with 
commercial uses, removing the possibility of the property developing with multi-family development. 
 
 
7. REVIEW – The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. 
  
Reviewing Agencies with Comments: 
- 
Development (Susana Montana) 
o 
(FYI) The CB zoning would best accommodate the proposed car wash footprint and 
building design.  
- 
Development (Seth Lloyd) 
o 
 (FYI) The parcel contained in this proposed zone change is suitable to develop in either 
the current MUB zone or the proposed CB zone. This property is also able to support the 
able to meet the intent and applicability of both zone districts. 
- 
Metro Planning Office (Tom Mason) 
o 
(FYI) College Dr. is a principal arterial and access should be taken from a lesser 
classified street. WYDOT will have the final say on where accesses will be allowed. 
- 
Urban Planning (Seth Lloyd) 
o 
(FYI) Property is designated as Mixed-use Commercial and as a Mixed-use Commercial 
Activity Center by PlanCheyenne. These areas are anticipated to develop primarily with a 
mix of commercial uses adjacent to arterial streets - with residential and civic uses as 
secondary uses.  
o 
(FYI) The MUB zone is the most appropriate zone for areas designated Mixed-use 
Commercial, but Mixed-use Activity Centers are anticipated to include either CB or MUB 
zoning. This parcel is adjacent to parcels zoned CB across College Drive. CB zoned 
parcels are also found within 1/4 mile to the southwest and north of this parcel. 
 
 

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00146-01

WYDOT (Randy Griesbach)

8. PUBLIC HEALTH, SAFETY, AND WELFARE – Whether the application will harm the public health, safety or 
general welfare. 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public health, 
safety, or general welfare.

PLANNING COMMISSION RECOMMENDATION:  At their regular meeting on June 4, 2018, the City 
of Cheyenne Planning Commission recommended approval of the zone change request from  
MR Medium-density Residential to MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis by a vote of 5-1. 
 
RECOMMENDATION:  Staff recommends approval of the zone change request from  
MR Medium-density Residential to MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis.

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00150 
3 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This zone change application proposes changing an entire block under the same ownership from MR 
Medium-density Residential to MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis. These lots total about 1.6 acres of 
land. This land is currently occupied by 16 dwelling units in 8 structures. The structures were originally 
built in the 1950’s. At the time the application for the zone change was submitted, the site was actively 
used as a residential facility. 
 
The applicant proposes a mix of multi-family and commercial uses at this site. The current MR zone only 
allows very limited commercial uses, and multi-family development requires a Conditional Use approval. 
The MUB zone allows a mix of uses, but expects that no individual block exceed 50% residential. 
Apartments and a variety of commercial uses are allowed in the MUB zone. 
 
As a smaller, walkable block near a commercial corridor and existing residential neighborhoods, this 
property meets the intent of the MUB zone district. Development patterns to the south include 
commercial development set close to the street with a higher design standard – although zoned PUD. 
Area currently zoned MUB is located nearby, but is not contiguous to this block. Contiguous zones do 
include a commercial PUD and CB Community Business zoning, but also LR Low-density Residential. 
 
Policies in PlanCheyenne both support and oppose the proposed zone change. PlanCheyenne encourages 
the maximization of existing infrastructure and MUB zoning in Mixed-use Activity Centers. PlanCheyenne 
also encourages the protection of existing residential neighborhoods and non-commercial zoning in areas 
designated Urban Residential.  
 
While MUB zoning is encouraged in Mixed-use Activity Centers, the location of the designated activity 
center is focused on the intersection of Western Hills/Storey and Yellowstone – making this property on 
the edge of the Activity Center. While the property is designated Urban Residential, property immediately 
adjacent is designated Mixed-use Commercial, and the designations on the Future Land Use Map are not 
intended to strictly pre-determine zones and uses. On the balance, Staff feels the proposed zone change 
is in accordance with the plans and policies outlined in PlanCheyenne 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of Section 2.2.1.d of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code, staff recommends approval of this zone change. 
 
 

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00150 
4

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00150 
6 
 
ZONE CHANGE DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions 
on a proposed Zone Change Request, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following criteria of Section 2.2.1.d: 
 
1. PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with 
the development policies and physical patterns identified 
in the Comprehensive Plan.  
 
PlanCheyenne is mixed on this zone change, but on the 
balance, Staff feels the proposed zone change is in 
accordance with the plans and policies outlined in 
PlanCheyenne. 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages maximization of existing 
infrastructure and redevelopment or revitalization of 
underutilized areas. This property is currently developed 
in an area where infrastructure is already installed. The 
current development on this property is a medium-high 
density of residential development. Properties near 
arterials are frequently developed with more intense 
uses – either commercial or higher-density residential. By 
rezoning this property to the MUB Mixed-use Business 
Emphasis zone, more intense uses could be established on this property. However, the maximization 
of existing infrastructure is not intended to encourage the redevelopment of all of Cheyenne to higher 
densities, as PlanCheyenne also encourages the stabilization and protection of older, established 
residential neighborhoods. The proposed change from MR to MUB would likely result in the 
redevelopment of an existing, 1950’s-era residential block. While development on this block dates 
from the 1950’s, the nearby residential properties across Main Street were developed (or 
redeveloped) in the 1990’s.  
 
PlanCheyenne designates the intersection of Western Hills/Storey and Yellowstone as the center of a 
Mixed-use Activity Center. Mixed-use Activity Centers are intended to develop with a mix of 
commercial uses with some housing. The CB and MUB zones are encouraged in these activity 
centers, and MUB zoning is proposed with this zone change. This property is on the edge of the 
Mixed-use Activity Center area, but still within a quarter-mile of the central intersection.  
 
This area is designated Urban Residential on the Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne. The MUB 
zone is generally not considered an appropriate zone for areas designated Urban Residential. Future 
Land Use designations on the Future Land Use Map for developed parcels often reflect the exiting 
development pattern. As the existing development pattern on this block is multi-family residential, 
the Urban Residential designation matches the existing development pattern well.  PlanCheyenne 
does mention that the Future Land Use Map is NOT intended to pre-determine uses, but is intended 
to illustrate general concepts for future development. The nearby future land use designation to the 
south is Mixed-use Commercial, which encourages a mix of commercial and residential development 
and suggests MUB zoning. 
Map #3 – Future Land Use  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00150 
7 
 
2. SURROUNDING AREA - The application is compatible with 
nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, the 
Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies 
created under the guidance of that plan for these areas.  
 
The proposed zoning designation is compatible with nearby 
properties as it matches some of the commercial zoning in 
the area and as nearby blocks include commercial-style 
development in the PUD and CB zones. The properties 
south and west of the PUD area are currently zoned MUB 
(although about 3½ acres of the area zoned MUB currently 
sits vacant). The Murray Office Park Second Filing PUD, 
approved in 1989, is developed with buildings set close to 
Prairie Hills Drive, and requires building design, screening, 
and landscaping. Development in the MUB zone would also 
require building design, landscaping, and structures set close 
to the street. While there is a large area zoned MUB less than 
100’ from this land, none of the 5 zone districts immediately adjacent to this area are zoned MUB. 
The perimeter of this zone change proposal is approximately 1400 linear feet, of this total 
approximately:  
• 
27.5% is contiguous to area currently zoned LR (west, across Main Street) 
• 
25.1% is contiguous to area currently zoned P (east, across Yellowstone Road – Davis 
Elementary School) 
• 
22.5% is contiguous to area currently zoned MR (north, across North Gate Avenue) 
• 
22.5% is contiguous to area currently zoned PUD (south, across Prairie Hills Drive) 
• 
2.4% is contiguous to area currently zoned CB (southeast, across Yellowstone Road) 
 
 
3. ZONING: BASIC STANDARDS & USES - The suitability of the subject property for development according 
to the standards and uses of the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject 
property for development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district.  
 
This property is currently zoned MR Medium-density Residential, which allows a variety of residential 
uses. However, the MR zone allows only very limited commercial activity (such as small offices). 
Apartments are allowed in the MR zone, but must receive conditional use approval. 
 
The proposed zone for this property is the MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis zone. This zone 
encourages a mix of residential and commercial uses. Multi-family is a use-by right in the MUB zone, 
as is a variety of commercial uses. MUB zoning requires additional design standards not generally 
applicable in the MR zone. The MUB zone does expect that no single MUB block would exceed 50% 
residential uses. On the associated plat application, the applicant has proposed an Option 1 which 
does not exceed this maximum; however, an Option 2 (also included with the plat) would create a 
block with 100% residential development. If zoned MR, the entire block could be developed with 
residential uses. 
 
Map #4 – Zoning Districts  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00150 
8 
 
4. SUBDIVISION STANDARDS - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
according to the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning.  
 
The applicant is proposing this zone change without proposing any changes to the streets or blocks. 
This neighborhood has an established street network. A plat is proposed concurrently with this zone 
change application which proposes redividing the lots into a new configuration. This zone change 
does not negatively affect the ability of this land and the surrounding neighborhood to meet any of 
the other standards found in Article 4. 
 
 
5. ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY, & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district, and achieve the Design Objectives of 
any specific standards. 
 
The MUB zone is intended to develop with a diverse mix of uses in an area with smaller, walkable 
blocks and compact development. Non-residential uses are anticipated to service nearby residential 
areas. MUB zoned areas should have a high degree of pedestrian amenities and meet a higher level 
of design standards. This property is on a smaller, walkable block with connections to a nearby 
residential neighborhood. Proximity to Yellowstone will encourage commercial development, and this 
property is likely able to support a mix of uses. 
 
 
6. ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in the 
event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval.  
 
The applicant is proposing rezoning this land with the intent to redevelop an existing block with a mix 
of multi-family residential and commercial uses. The proposed development is able to meet all 
standards of the proposed zone district. As this block is a smaller, walkable block, a variety of 
development options meeting MUB zoning intent, applicability, and standards is possible in the event 
the applicant’s specific proposal is not executed. 
 
 
7. REVIEW – The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. 
  
Reviewing Agencies with Comments: 
- 
Development Department (Seth Lloyd)  
o 
(FYI) The MUB zoning applies the lot types of the NR-3 zone for residential development 
and the NB zone for commercial development. Larger commercial or mixed-use lots may 
also utilize the MUB lot type. 
o 
(FYI) Commercial and mixed-use lots must meet the standards of UDC 6.7 Small-scale 
Mixed-use and Commercial Design Standards. 
o 
(FYI) Residential lots must meet the standards of UDC 6.6 Residential Design Standards. 
- 
Urban Planning (Seth Lloyd) 
o 
(FYI) This area is designated Urban Residential and near a Mixed-use Activity Center on 
the Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne. The MUB zone is one of the appropriate 

Zone Change Application/UDC-18-00150 
9 
zoning options for Mixed-use Activity Centers, but is generally not considered an 
appropriate zone for areas designated Urban Residential. This block may have been 
designated Urban Residential based on the current residential development on-site. 
o 
(FYI) PlanCheyenne encourages maximization of existing infrastructure and 
redevelopment or revitalization of underutilized areas. The MUB zone would allow more 
dense and varied uses over the current MR zone. 
o 
(FYI) PlanCheyenne also encourages the stabilization and protection of older, 
established residential neighborhoods. The proposed change from MR to MUB would 
likely result in the redevelopment of an existing, 1950’s-era residential neighborhood. 
Reviewing Agencies without Comments: 
- 
City Engineer (Amy Allen) 
- 
Development (Susana Montana) 
- 
Engineering Department – General (Mark Escobedo) 
- 
Engineering Department – GIS/Tech (Gary Stogsdill) 
- 
Metro Planning Office (Tom Mason & Nancy Olson) 
- 
Public Works (Nathan Beauheim) 
- 
Urban Planning (Richard Zita) 
Reviewing Agencies not Responding: 
- 
None 
Other Comments Received: 
- 
None 
 
8. PUBLIC HEALTH, SAFETY, AND WELFARE – Whether the application will harm the public health, safety or 
general welfare. 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public health, 
safety, or general welfare.
