# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_60839993.pdf
Approved as to
form only:
RESOLUTION NO.
Date:i1!53i5
ENTITLED:
“A RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE CONTINUATION OF THE OPTIONAL ONE
PERCENT SALES TAX FOR CALENDAR YEARS 2019 THROUGH 2022 AND
PLEDGING THAT ALL REVENUES DERIVED FROM THIS TAX WILL BE USED
FOR PROJECTS DESCRIBED IN THIS RESOLUTION.”
WHEREAS, Wyoming statutes allow cities and counties in Wyoming to impose an Optional One
Percent Sales Tax; and
WHEREAS, the Optional One Percent Sales Tax has been in effect in the City of Cheyenne and
Laramie County since 197$ and has been used to finance many improvements in the county and its
communities, primarily for the rebuilding, upgrading and maintenance of county roads, city streets and
traffic safety systems; and
WHEREAS, the efficiency and response capabilities of the City Fire & Rescue and Police
Departments have been dramatically upgraded and the tax continues to be necessary to allow these
Departments to continue to provide vehicles and equipment for public safety projects and services; and
WHEREAS, the health and safety of the community and federal rules dictate that storm water
drainage systems be maintained, developed, and improved; and
WHEREAS, the Public Works Department, Community Recreation & Events Department,
the City County Health Department, the Cheyenne Animal Shelter, Human Service Agencies, the
Cheyenne Airport Board, and the Cheyenne- Laramie County Economic Development Joint Powers
Board need the funding available through the continuation of this Optional One Percent Sales Tax to
provide services and upgrade facilities; and
WHEREAS, this funding has been instrumental to economic development which brings jobs to
Cheyenne; and
WHEREAS, the cost of the necessary improvements, facilities, equipment and services is well
beyond the capabilities of the City of Cheyenne without the continuation of the Optional One Percent
Sales Tax; and
WHEREAS, it has been the practice of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to define the
purposes for which these tax revenues would be used and to recognize that anticipated improvements and
expenditures are subject to availability of funds; and
WHEREAS, the elected officials are accountable for the use of these revenues; and
WHEREAS, accountability is dependent on verification of optional sales tax expenditures and
interpretation of this resolution.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that it supports the passage of the Optional One Percent Sales Tax for
calendar years 2019 through 2022.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that if the Optional One Percent Sales Tax is renewed, the revenues
received are to be placed into a special fund to be used solely for one percent sales tax projects.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the following list designates projects that are to be funded from
the Optional One Percent Sales Tax:
1

2 
 
STREET AND ROAD MAINTENANCE AND CONSTRUCTION:  $34,000,000 (80%) 
ESTIMATED 
COST 
Project Name and Location 
(Items 1 through 6 not listed in priority sequence) 
1. 
$18,200,000 
City street and pavement maintenance and improvements, including construc- 
tion, reconstruction, renovation, and grant match for maintenance projects (i.e. 
overlay, chip/slurry/micro/crack seals, traffic signals, and concrete work). These 
funds are necessary to ensure that the condition of city pavements can be 
maintained and improved to preclude more costly total reconstruction. 
2. 
$4,500,000 
Snow removal, street cleaning, and street maintenance materials (i.e. asphalt 
mix, sealers, ice melt material, sand, salt, gravel), equipment, facilities, and 
grant match for materials, equipment, and facilities. 
3. 
$2,900,000 
Traffic Safety Improvements including replacing/upgrading traffic signals, 
signal controllers and signs, grant match for traffic improvements, and funding 
for traffic signs, posts, paint, striping, equipment, and facilities. 
4. 
$2,200,000 
Administration and management of one percent sales tax projects. Partially 
funds administrative costs of project management, construction management, 
engineering, pavement management and project communications with the 
public. 
5. 
$2,200,000 
Construction, maintenance, and repair of storm sewers, culverts, drainage 
channels, detention ponds, and grant match for drainage projects. This funding 
is necessary to ensure routine repair of drainage facilities preventing damage to 
public and private property while meeting federal requirements including costs 
associated with municipal separate storm sewer systems (MS4) permitting. 
6. 
$4,000,000 
Funding designated for projects to be designed and constructed as listed 
below.  This funding may be used for reconstruction, widening, resurfacing, 
drainage repairs and improvements, rehabilitation, improvements to 
accommodate increased traffic, state or federal grant match for projects, or other 
improvements deemed necessary by the City Engineer. Funding to complete a 
priority project will be secured and verified prior to making remaining funds 
available for the next priority project, other road projects, or road maintenance. 
 
Priority 1 
Replace and widen bridge on 5th Street over Crow Creek.  Needed to 
accommodate increased traffic volumes and improve pedestrian safety and 
connectivity. 
 
Priority 2 
Widen East Dell Range between College Drive and Whitney Road.  Needed to 
accommodate increased traffic volumes.  Will compliment County and State 
projects currently in the planning process. 
 
Priority 3 
Realign Missile Drive at 19th Street.  Adds new bridge over Crow Creek and 
improves safety by providing perpendicular intersection and adding pedestrian 
corridor. 

3 
 
OTHER PROJECTS AND AGENCIES:  $8,500,000 (20%) 
 
$1,233,000 (2.901%)  Fire and Rescue Department for fire protection and training facilities 
maintenance and development, fire equipment and emergency medical 
equipment and supplies, communications equipment, training, protective 
clothing, medical evaluations, computer and software upgrades, vehicles and 
matching grant opportunities. This funding is critical in ensuring firefighters are 
trained and equipped to provide fire and rescue protection services. 
 
$2,165,000 (5.094%) Police Department Public Safety and Protection Projects, including the 
replacement and equipping of police cars, upgrading communication 
equipment, purchasing needed equipment for training and investigations, 
replacing outdated computers and software, upgrading facilities and matching 
grant opportunities. This funding is critical in ensuring our police are properly 
equipped to provide public safety. 
 
$400,000 
(0.941%) Community Recreation & Events Department Right-of-Way Maintenance for 
routine upkeep, preservation, and replacement of landscape enhanced City 
rights-of­ way. Costs include equipment, materials, supplies, and labor for 
tree pruning, tree replacement, snow removal, mowing, weed control, debris 
removal and irrigation. This funding is needed to provide safe and well-
maintained landscaping in City rights-of-way. 
 
$1,573,000 (3.701%) Community Recreation & Events Department Facilities, Equipment, 
Grounds and Programs. This funding is needed to maintain public health, 
safety, and enhancement of Cheyenne's parks and recreational resources. This 
funding may also be used to meet grant fund matching requirements. 
 
$717,000 (1.687%)  Equipment, vehicles, facility repairs, computers, software, and their related 
costs of installation/implementation for various City and associated agencies 
excluding Street & Alley, Traffic, Police, Fire and Community Recreation & 
Events. Department/agency heads submit requests for this funding to the 
Mayor annually. 
 
$282,000 
(0.664%) City County Health Department. This funding is needed to maintain and replace 
vehicles and equipment and provide mileage reimbursement to employees who 
drive their privately-owned vehicles to complete their assigned duties. 
 
$90,000 
(0.212%) Animal Shelter. This funding is needed to maintain and replace vehicles and 
equipment. 
 
$240,000 
(0.565%) Airport Board.  This funding is used to pay annual lease contracts for properties 
where the following facilities are located: Weed and Pest Operations ($3,750), 
Compost Facility ($32,812), and Community Recreation & Events Converse 
Softball and Baseball Fields ($23,438). 
 
$1,000,000 (2.353%) Human Services Agencies.  These funds provide direct financial support to 
local agencies as recommended by the Human Services Advisory Council 
(HSAC) and approved by the governing body. The HSAC is a joint advisory 
council consisting of City, County and United Way appointees created to provide 
recommendations to each governing body concerning human services matters. This 
process provides agencies a single application, review and hearing process for 
funding requests and develops a system to monitor effectiveness of funds 
committed. 
 
$800,000 
(1.882%) Cheyenne-Laramie County Joint Powers Economic Development Board. 
These funds are used to assist with the expansion, retention and recruitment 
of businesses by providing financial incentives and infrastructure so that 
Cheyenne and Laramie County can diversify the economy by retaining and 
creating jobs. The purpose of the Cheyenne-Laramie County Economic 

4 
 
Development Joint Powers Board is to enhance the economy of Cheyenne and 
Laramie County. The Board may use the optional one percent tax to assist 
Cheyenne LEADS by providing funding for business recruitment and infrastructure 
development; sponsor reports or publications that provide economic, business, or 
industry data or market reports; and provide financial incentives or funding of 
special projects to encourage businesses to expand or relocate to Cheyenne and 
Laramie County. These funds may be used as grant match for associated 
opportunities, full or partial grant, or loaned out upon approval by the Board. 
 
Total of all Projects under City's Share of the Optional One Percent Sales Tax for calendar years 
2019 through 2022 inclusive:  $42,500,000 (100%) 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that interest earned from Optional One Percent Sales Tax funds 
will be used to pay administration and management costs of one percent sales tax projects and pay 
$10,500 of the costs of United Way administering Human Services Agency funding. Designated project 
funding saved by performing in-house engineering design or construction management may be used to 
offset administration and management costs of one percent sales tax projects. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that all funds realized from the 2019-2022 Optional One Percent 
Sales Tax shall be spent to complete one percent sales tax projects. Funds collected from the Optional 
One Percent Sales Tax which exceed the estimate of $42,500,000 are for the purposes of this resolution 
described as "overage funds." Overage funds shall first be used to pay administration and management 
costs of one percent sales tax projects, if any, not covered by other funds allocated in this resolution. 
Remaining overage funds will be distributed as follows: 80% shall be allocated to Street and Road 
Maintenance, Drainage, and Construction projects listed on page 2 of this resolution; 5% shall be 
allocated to the Public Works Department for City Operations and Maintenance Projects; and 15% shall 
be allocated to Other Projects and Agencies in the following percentages for use as described on page 3 of 
this resolution: Fire and Rescue Department 17.46%; Police Department 30.67%; Community Recreation 
& Events Department 22.28%; Equipment and Facilities for Various City Agencies 10.16%; City County 
Health Department 3.99%; Animal Shelter 1.28%; and Human Services 14.16%. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that if revenue from the Optional One Percent Sales Tax is below 
the $42,500,000 estimate, the governing body will hold a public meeting and reallocate funding by 
resolution. 
 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED ON THIS ____   DAY OF __________ , 2018. 
 
 
 
                                                                                _______________________________________ 
                                                                                Marian J. Orr, Mayor 
 
(SEAL)  
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
________________________________________ 
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_48370817.pdf
Approved as to
rm only:
Date:.
3
RESOLUTION NO.
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION TO ESTABLISH UNIFORM PROCEDURES, FEES,
COSTS, AND CHARGES FOR INSPECTING, COPYING, AN]) PRODUCING
PUBLIC RECORDS.”
WHEREAS, it is in the best interest of the City of Cheyenne to establish a uniform
procedure for its departments and workforce, including a delineation of fees, costs, and charges for
inspecting, copying, and producing public records; and
WHEREAS, the Wyoming Public Records Act, Wyoming Statutes §* 16-4-201 through 16-
4-205, provides authority and sets forth procedures for the City to follow with regard to responding
to requests for public records; and
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 16-4-204(c) requires any fees or charges assessed by a
custodian of public records to “first be authorized by duly enacted or adopted statute, rule,
resolution, ordinance, executive order or other like authority”; and
WHEREAS, the following definitions, procedures and fees shall apply when City staff
receives and responds to requests for public records:
1.
Definitions.
a.
“Applicant” is the person requesting the public records.
b.
“Clerical/support staff’
are
employees
who
generally perform office
or
administrative
support duties.
Clerical/support staff employees
include
secretaries
and
administrative assistants.
c.
“Custodian” means the authorized City staff having personal custody and control
of the public records in question.
d.
“Electronic/digital public record” is a public record that is primarily or solely
stored in an electronic/digital format. Typically, the custodian will only be able to produce a copy
ofthe original electronic/digital public record due to the native format, security, and integrity ofthe
original data or electronic record.
e.
“Information technology staff’ are employees who perform duties relating to
retrieving, compiling, constructing, formatting, or extracting electronic public records located on
computer systems, software, servers, or networks. Information technology staff may also perform
computer programming or other computer services relating to electronic public records.
f.
“Professional staff’ are employees who are not clerical/support or information
technology staff as defined herein.
Professional staff employees perform administrative,
managerial, or professional duties.
g.
“Supervised copying” occurs ifsomeone otherthan the custodian is allowed to make
copies, printouts, or photographs.
2.
Reouest for Public Records.
a.
A request for public records shall be submitted in writing by the applicant either on
forms provided by the custodian or in a written format that contains sufficient information for the
custodian to determine the completeness of the information being request. A request may also be
submitted via electronic communication (Le. e-mail) or facsimile transmission.
b.
The custodian shall acknowledge in writing receipt of the request within seven (7)
business days of the request.
I

3.
Electronic/Digital Public Records.
a.
Production and Construction Costs. Pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 16-4-202(d)(i),
the custodian shall charge an applicant the reasonable costs ofproducing and constructing a copy
of an electronic public record for inspection and copying. This cost may include, but is not limited
to, the time spent retrieving, compiling, sorting, reviewing, redacting, formatting, converting, or
copying the electronic public record, as well as activities required to create or construct a new
electronic public record from existing data sources and all associated programming and computer
services.
b.
Production and Construction Costs. Production and construction costs for electronic
public records shall be as follows:
i.
$20.50/hour for clerical stafftime.
ii.
$34.00/hour for information technology staff time.
iii.
$50.00/hour for professional staff time.
iv.
Actual cost of programming and computer services.
c.
Payment.
The custodian shall provide the applicant with an estimate of the
reasonable costs ofproduction and construction ofthe electronic public records. The applicant shall
pre-pay the estimated costs before the custodian produces or constructs the electronic public records
or provides any copies for inspection. Payment shall be made to the custodian. If the final cost of
producing and constructing the record exceed the original estimate, the custodian shall noti1’ the
applicant and seek additional payment.
d.
Refund. If the actual costs ofproducing or constructing the electronic public record
is less than the amount estimated and paid, the custodian shall refund the excess payment at the same
time the applicant is allowed to inspect or retrieve the electronic/digital public records.
e.
Inspection. The custodian shall noti& the applicant in writing when copies of the
electronic public records are produced and available for inspection. The applicant shall have one
month from the time the custodian provides notification to inspect the records at the custodian’s
designated location. After the one-month time period, the request shall be officially closed.
f.
Costs for Producing Copies. If the applicant also requests printed copies of the
electronic public record, the fee schedule set forth in Section 4 shall also apply.
4.
Non-Electronic/Digital Public Records.
a.
Inspection. The custodian shall noti& the applicant in writing when copies of the
non-electronic/digital public records are produced and available for inspection. The applicant shall
have one month from the time the custodian provides notification to come to the custodian’s
designated location to inspect the records. After the one-month time period, the request shall be
officially closed.
b.
Fees for Copyine Non-ElectronicfDiQital Public Records. An applicant may obtain
a paper copy of a non-electronic/digital public record upon payment as follows:
i.
Standard (8.5” x 11”)— Black and White Copy
$0.1 0/page
ii.
Standard (8.5” x 11”)— Colored Copy
$0.60/page
iii.
Legal (8.5” x 14”) —Black and White Copy
$0.25/page
iv.
Legal (8.5” x 14”)—Colored Copy
$1.00/page
v.
Ledger (11” x 17”)— Black and White Copy
$0.50/page
2

vi.
Ledger (11” x 17”)— Colored Copy
$2.00/page
vii.
24” x 36”— Black and White Copy
$1.00/page
viii.
24” x 36”
— Colored Copy
$2.50/page
ix.
Other sheet size
Actual Cost
x.
Photograph
Actual Cost
xi.
Utilization of an outside vendor for copying
Actual Cost
xii.
Custodian’s fee for supervised copying
See section 3(b)
xiii.
Special instances, Le., film
Actual Cost
c.
Payment.
The custodian shall provide the applicant with an estimate of the
reasonable costs ofproduction and construction ofthe electronic public records. The applicant shall
pre-pay the estimated costs before the custodian reproduces the public records. Payment shall be
made to the custodian. Ifthe final cost ofproducing and constructing the record exceed the original
estimate, the custodian shall noti& the applicant and seek additional payment.
d.
Reffind. If the actual costs of reproducing the public record is less than the amount
estimated and paid, the custodian shall refirnd the excess payment at the same time the applicant
retrieves the public records.
e.
Costs for ProducinE Electronic Copies. An applicant may obtain an electronic copy
of a non-electronic public record upon payment as follows:
i.
Payment for staff time, see Section 3 above; and
ii.
Scanning non-electronic public records for
e-mail or facsimile transmission
$0.10/page
iii.
Electronic Media (disk, thumb drive, etc.)
Actual Cost
f.
Fees for Transmiftin Public Records. The custodian may charge the following fees
for transmitting non-electronic/digital public records:
i.
Shipping fee and cost of the shipping container
Actual Cost
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne adopts this Resolution for the purpose of
establishing a uniform procedure for its departments and workforce, and the delineation of fees,
costs and charges for inspecting, copying, and producing public records; and
BE IT HEREBY FURTHER RESOLVED, that nothing in this Resolution shall be construed
as authorizing a fee to be charged as a condition of making a public record available for public
inspection.

day of

RESOLUTION NO.

FEE
SCHEDULE
FOR

AND

WHEREAS, Wyo. Stat. § 35-1-305(a) authorizes local boards of health to fix and charge
reasonable fees for services; and
WhEREAS, the Cheyenne-Laramie County Board ofHealth currently provides inspection
services for food service operations, body art, childcare facilities, campgrounds, small wastewater
systems, swimming pools/spas, massage establishments and therapists, non-hazardous liquid waste
haulers, and rural property; and
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne-Laramie County Board ofHealth wishes to amend its health fee
schedule for services, inspections and permitting as previously adopted by City Resolution No. 5680,
approved by the governing body on May 8, 2015, by revising the fee schedule for certain services,
inspections and permits in accordance with the attached Exhibit A; and
WHEREAS, the public hearing required by Wyo. Stat. § 35-1-305(c) was conducted on May
15, 2018, by the Cheyenne-Laramie County Board of Health; and
WHEREAS, prior to services, permit and inspection fees being assessed, the fee schedule
must be adopted by the City of Cheyenne and LaramIe County Board of Commissioners for their
respective jurisdictions.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the revised health fee schedule for inspection services,
permit fees and other various services provided by the Cheyenne-Laramie County Board ofHealth,
which schedule is set forth in Exhibit A to this resolution, is hereby adopted.
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this
day of
, 201$

RESOLUTION NO.
ENTITLED:
“A RESOLUTION APPROVING SANITARY SEWER SERVICE, BY USER’S
AGREEMENT, WITH ROCKY MOUNTAIN REI, LLC. A COUNTY TRACT IN
SE ¼, Sw ¼, SECTION 26, TOWNSHIP 14 NORTH, RANGE 66 WEST OF THE
6TH• P.M., LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING.”
WHEREAS, Rocky Mountain REI, LLC, is the owners of the property located at and
described as follows:
A County Tract in SE 1/4, SW 1/4, Section 26, Township 14 North, Range 66 West
of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming; and
WHEREAS, the Property owners desire to connect to the sanitary sewer service pursuant to a
User’s Agreement; and
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution No. 3577, the Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) was
instructed to present to the Governing Body for its review any application for the provision of
sanitary sewer service to any individual or entity outside the corporate limits of the City of
Cheyenne; and
WHEREAS, the BOPU approved the sanitary sewer service application provided the
Property owner Rocky Mountain REI, LLC, signed a User’s agreement pending approval by the
Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne pursuant to Resolution No. 3577; and
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has reviewed the request for sanitary sewer service and
determined that approval is in the best interest of the City.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT THE APPLICATION FOR SANITARY SEWER SERVICE TO
THE PROPERTY IS HEREBY APPROVED FOR ROCKY MOUNTAIN REl, LLC, A COUNTY
TRACT IN SE 1/4, SW 1/4, SECTION 26, TOWNSHiP 14 NORTH, RANGE 66 WEST OF THE
6TH P.M., LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING.
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS
DAY OF
,20___
Marian Orr, Mayor
(SEAL)
ATTEST:
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk
Dev Proc,Outside City,User Agreement,Resol

OUTSIDE USER SANITARY SEWER
SERVICES AGREEMENT
This agreement is made this l
day of
cpdl
, 20!
between the
Board ofPublic Utilities ofthe City ofCheyenne, a municipal corporation organized pursuant to the
laws ofthe State ofWyoming, hereinafter referred to as the “BOPU,” and
ièc
Q6J’ ,LL- L__
hereinafter referred to as the “USER(S).”
WITNESSETH:
WHEREAS, the USER(S) are the current owners of and are desirous of connecting
the property described as follows to BOPU sanitary sewer:
Rocky Mountain REI, LLC.
3304 Hayes Avenue, Cheyenne, WY. 82001
A County Tract in SE 1/4, SW 1/4, Section 26, Township 14 North, Range 66 West
of the 6” P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming.
WHEREAS, the BOPU has exclusive control of all City of Cheyenne, (hereinafter,
“City”), owned sanitary sewer systems and operates the same for the City and may furnish surplus
sanitary sewer facilities to persons outside the City pursuant to WYO. STAT. § 15-7-404 (a) (1977)
and Title 13 of the Cheyenne City Code.
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration ofthe mutual promises and covenants herein
contained, the parties agree as follows:
1.
The BOPU shall allow the USER(S) to connect the above-referenced property to the
existing BOPU sanitary sewer system at points determined by the BOPU.
2.
The BOPU shall provide all necessary inspections for the construction and installation
of the service line connections.
The USER(S) shall provide the BOPU with a certificate of
compliance, signed in affidavit form by a contractor licensed in the City, verifying that all
construction materials and trade installation of service lines, service line fittings, and termination
points at or in USER(S) dwellings and at the location ofthe USER’(S) dwellings are in accordance
with applicable provisions offederal, state and local codes, laws, and/or ordinances. Specific codes
in effect include BOPU’S standards, International Plumbing Codes and International Mechanical
Codes. Specific reference shall be given stating that the service line is only servicing one building.
Inspection fees to be paid by the USER(S) shall compensate the BOPU at 1.5 times the then current
inspection fee charged to in-City users.
3.
The USER(S) shall retain ownership of all improvements from the point oftapping
into the sewer main and shall be responsible for the maintenance, repair and upkeep thereof.
4.
The USER(S) shall comply with all applicable federal, state and local laws, rules,
regulations and ordinances governing sanitary sewer systems including but not limited to the
applicable chapters of Cheyenne City Code Title 13. The USER(S) shall comply with all technical
codes applicable to in-City construction which use or connect to the sewer main including the BOPU
standards, International Plumbing Codes and International Mechanical Codes.
5.
The USER(S) shall pay 1.5 times the BOPU sewer rates presently in effect for in
CITY users and shall be subject to all subsequent changes thereof. The USER(S) shall pay 1.5 times
the BOPU sewer tap fees, system development fees and all other appropriate rates and fees. In the
event the BOPU negotiates a different sewer rate with a similarly situated user, the BOPU and
USER(S) agree to adjust the sewer rates in a manner consistent with that negotiated with the

with the sewer rates charged to the customers in said district by the sewer district. If annexation to
the City takes place, the sewer rate and tap fees and other such charges will be not more than those
charged to all other BOPU users. USER(S) charges for residential contributors not connected to City
water shall be based on an average water consumption for that type of user, as determined by the
BOPU. Ifthe USER(S) is an industrial or commercial user and is not connected to BOPU water and
utilizing the same for domestic purposes, to compute monthly sewer charges, USER(S) shall install a
water meter on their domestic water supply pursuant to the following conditions:
a)
the BOPU shall furnish required meters which shall remain the property of the
BOPU;
b)
the USER(S) shall provide the necessary meter vault and plumbing such that a meter
can be installed;
c)
the USER(S) shall install the meter;
d)
the BOPU shall maintain and read the meter from which monthly sewer charges will
be calculated; and
e)
the USER(S) may meter irrigation water separate from domestic and commercial
use for calculating sewer charges.
6.
The USER(S) hereby consents to the annexation ofthe above-referenced property in
the event said property qualifies for annexation to the City in accordance with WYO. STAT. § 15-1 -
402 and Cheyenne City Code Chapter 1.16 as the same may be hereafter amended and, by warranty
deed covenants, will
require future USER(S) to consent to the terms and conditions of this
agreement including the annexation ofthe above-referenced property which utilizes the sewer mains
in the event said property qualifies for annexation to the City in accordance with WYO. STAT. 15-1-
402 and Cheyenne City Code Chapter 1.16 as the same may be hereafter amended.
7.
No septic systems will be allowed within said property so long as sanitary sewer
service is furnished by the BOPU or its successors.
8.
The USER(S) shall include the affirmative covenant attached hereto as Exhibit A and
made a part hereofby this reference, with appropriate book and page numbers from the filing ofthis
agreement with the Laramie County Clerk ofDeeds, in all deeds from USER(S) conveying property
included in the above-referenced property and hereby assign an equal right to the City to enforce the
same.
9.
The BOPU reserves the right to increase said sewer rate (to 2.0 times the in-City rate
or the maximum allowed by law) to any property or lot within the above-referenced property not
annexed to the City if said property or lot qualifies for annexation to the City and the City requests
such annexation.
10.
The BOPU and City ofCheyenne reserve all governmental/sovereign immunities as
provided by law.
The USER(S) shall indemnify and hold harmless the BOPU and CITY, their
officers, agents, employees and representatives from any claim or liability arising out ofthe failure to
comply with the terms and conditions of this agreement or from any other action regarding the
construction of said sanitary sewer system.
11. If a provision, section, subsection, sentence, clause or phrase of this agreement is
invalidated by any court of competent jurisdiction, such holding shall not affect the validity ofthe
remainder of the agreement, which shall continue in full force and effect.
12.
The terms and conditions contained herein shall be a covenant running with the land
and shall extend to and be binding upon the USER(S), their heirs, devisees, personal representatives,
successors and assigns.
13.
This agreement contains all the agreements ofthe representations between the parties.
None of the terms of this agreement shall be waived or modified to any extent, except by written
instrument signed and delivered by both parties.

iN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties hereto have executed this agreement on the
date first above written.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_51823205.pdf
rm only:
RESOLUTION NO.
Date:
S
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION TO ESTABLISH UNIFORM PROCEDURES, FEES,
COSTS AND CHARGES FOR INSPECTING, COPYING AND PRODUCING
PUBLIC RECORDS.”
WHEREAS, the City ofCheyenne acknowledges its citizens’ rights to access public records,
and to obtain such records pursuant to Wyoming law; and
WHEREAS, the Wyoming Public Records Act, Wyoming Statutes § § 16-4-201 through 16-
4-205, provides authority and sets forth procedures for the City to follow with regard to responding
to requests for public records; and
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 164-204(c) requires any fees or charges assessed by a
custodian of public records to “first be authorized by duly enacted or adopted statute, rule,
resolution, ordinance, executive order or other like authority”;
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the following fee schedule be established for all requests for
public records; and
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that nothing in this resolution shall be construed as
authorizing a fee to be charged as a condition of making a public record available for public
inspection.
t.
Copy char2cs and related fees.
Service
Fee
Black and white copies
$1.00 l pg. + $0.25 each additional
Color copies
$1.00/pg. or cost to contract out
Photograph
Actual cost
Fax transmission
SI .00/pg.
Police reports
Per department policy
Use of outside vendor for copying
Actual cost
Special instances, Le., film
Actual cost
Electronic media (disk, thumb drive, etc.)
Actual cost
Postage/shipping
Actual cost
Research or compilation services
Actual staff time cost
2.
Electronic/Digital Public Records.
Wyoming Statute §
1 6-4-202(d)(i) allows a fee to be charged for the reasonable cost of
producing and constructing a copy of an electronic public record for inspection and copying. The
City may charge an applicant the actual cost of any programming and computer services for such
service plus any copy or electronic media fees listed above.
3.
The City of Cheyenne is authorized to use discretion in assessing the necessity and amount
of fees when charging for requests. Additional charges may be assessed on an as-needed basis at
the discretion of the Mayor or appropriate Department Director.
4.
The Mayor or appropriate Department Director may exercise discretion in determining fees
that are not listed on this schedule.
5.
This fee schedule is not applicable to governmental entities and is not intended for
interdepartmental use.
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED on this
_____
day of
,2018.
Marian J. On, Mayor
(SEAL)
Attest:
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk

WHEREAS, the Downtown Development Authority (“DDA”) was created pursuant to
Ordinance No. 2804 adopted by the Governing Body on July 9, 1984; and
WHEREAS, Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-208(a) requires the DDA to propose a budget, which shall
be administratively reviewed by the City prior to submission of the proposed budget to the
Governing Body for approval; and

be subject to alteration, modification and approval by the Governing Body in accordance with the
policies of the City; and

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the proposed budget for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2018
and ending June 30, 2019 of the Downtown Development Authority, as set forth in the attached
information with this Resolution entitled “Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority, Fiscal

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_69055064.pdf
Approved as to
RESOLUTION NO.
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION APPROVING THE BUDGET OF THE CHEYENNE
REGIONAL AIRPORT BOARD FOR THE FISCAL YEAR BEGINNING
JULY 1, 2018 AND ENDING JUNE 30, 2019.”
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board (“Board”) was created pursuant to
Ordinance No. 2407 adopted by the Governing Body on February 11, 1980, as amended; and
WHEREAS, City Code Section 2.60.050(B) sets forth the requirement that the Board shall
submit on or before the first day of May of each year, to the governing bodies of the city and the
county, a proposed budget for the ensuing fiscal year; and
WHEREAS, City Code Section 2.60.050(B) provides that the Board budget shall be subject
to alteration, modification and approval by the Governing Body in accordance with the policies of
the City; and
WHEREAS, City staff has reviewed the proposed budget received from the Board.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the proposed budget for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1,2018
and ending June 30, 2019 of the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board, as set forth in the attached
information with this Resolution entitled “Cheyenne Regional Airport Proposed 2018/2019
Operating Budget” in the total amount of $29,063,729.00, is hereby approved.
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this
_____day
of
, 2018.
Marian J. Orr, Mayor
(SEAL)
Attest:
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk

CHEYENNE
P.O. Box 2210
Cheyenne, WY 82003
www.flycheyenne.net
DATE:
TO:
FROM:
May 1, 2018
MEMORANDUM
SUBJECT:
Cheyenne Regional Airport Proposed 20 18/19 Operating Budget
On behalf of the Cheyenne Regional Airport, we would like to take this opportunity to present
to the City of Cheyenne the proposed 2018/19 Airport Operating Budget. Realizing this is the
first time for many of the Council to review the budget, we wanted to take a few brief moments
to explain the budget so you can understand the document as you are reviewing it.
BUDGET FLOW
Not unlike the City, the Airport is a large complex organization, just with fewer employees. For
budgeting purposes, the Airport is essentially broken into twelve (12) different departments or
Cost Centers as identified horizontally across the top of the budget worksheet. The
Departments (Cost Centers) are allocated by the following:
10- Airfield,
20- Existing Terminal,
25- New Terminal
30- General Aviation
35- Fixed Base Operation
40— Federal Government Buildings
50
— Air Traffic
60- Airport Administration
65
— Old Administration
70- Maintenance
80— Air Park (FBI & Great Lakes)
90- Parkway (Professional Building)
From these Budget Headers, each Cost Center’s line item allocation is on the left of the
document and takes the viewer through each line item across Cost Center lines.
Similar to the City, each of the Airport’s Cost Centers are further broken down according to
GAAP in that they show Operating and Non-Operating Revenue, Capital Outlays, Matching
Funds and Federal and State Grants. However, the budget is presented on a cash basis and
T
Ph: 307/634-7071
Fax: 307/632-1206
www.cheyenneairport.com
City of Cheyenne, The Honorable Marian Off, Mayor
Tim Barth, Director of Aviation, Cheyenne Regional Airport 7,,
Judy Clemons, Director of Finance & HR 2c

excludes accrual items like depreciation. It should also be noted that Airport Revenue can only
be spent in accordance with Federal guidelines given the amount of Federal investment in the
Airport.
BUDGET SUMMARY
Operating Revenues - are predicted for this budget cycle to be $4,289,508. **lt must be noted that
Operating Revenues will vary based on Airport Operations as the amount of fuel, de-icing fluid, line
service tasks, etc., vary by the number of aircraft received from private, military and commercial.
It should also be noted that Operating Revenues are anticipated to be down $270,650 now that Great
Lakes Airlines has ceased operations.
Non-Operating Revenue
— are projected at $12,174,916. This number reflects the majority of the costs
associated with the New Terminal Project and are carried over in this budget cycle to complete the
terminal. It should be noted that the majority of this funding is derived from 5th g 6th Penny Taxes,
Federal, State and Wyoming Business Council grants.
Compensation & Benefits
— are projected at $1,958, 230. The Airport currently employs 17 FTE’s and 3
PTE’s.
AIRPORT & FBO Supplies
— are projected at $1,652,656. It should be noted the largest expense in this
category is the bulk purchase of aviation fuels for resale.
Contract Service
— are projected at $884,793. These costs are primarily attributed to Airport legal
counsel, insurance, printing, telecommunications, web based applications, accounting services and
supplemental lawn and snow removal services.
Capital Outlays
— are projected at $7,748,338. It should be noted that the majority of these expenses
are offset with agency grants.
Debt Service — are projected at $355,288. This debt service pertains to the Federal Government building
the Airport built for the various agencies and are recouping the debt payments through existing leases
according to the agreements.
BUDGET PROFIT/LOSS
— Anticipated $598,129 will be returned to the Airport Reserve account for
consideration in the 2019/20 Operating Budget.
CONCLUSION
Since the Cheyenne Regional Airport Joint Powers Board was formed in 1980, the Airport has not been
an ongoing line item in either the City or County operating budgets. The Airport is completely self
sustaining and covers all expenses associated with the operations and maintenance of the facility.
For additional reference, we have attached City Ordinance 2426 which delineates the JPB agreement
between the City, County and Airport Board.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2018-06-01_TotalExtraction_18562603.pdf
Approved as to
RESOLUTION NO.
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE EXPEDIENT REDEVELOPMENT
OF THE BUILDING LOCATED AT 1602 CAPITOL AVENUE, ChEYENNE,
WYOMING, COMMONLY REFERRED TO AS THE HISTORIC RYNDS
BUILDING, AND THE LOT ADJACENT TO AND WEST OF THE HYNDS
BUILDING KNOWN AS THE HOLE.”
WHEREAS, on or about February 27, 2017, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne
(City) adopted Resolution No. 5835, stating its commitment to spend up to Seven Hundred Fifty
Thousand Dollars ($750,000.00) toward the development of the Hole in order to provide parking
for the H3mds Building located at 1602 Capitol Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (the Hynds);
WI-WREAS, the Historic Hynds Building is located on a site previously owned by former
Wyoming Governor, United States Senator, and accomplished businessman, Francis E. Warren, who
conveyed the she to Harry P. Hynds in 1917; and
WHEREAS, the Hynds was constructed as a state-of-the-art office building in 1919 by the
distinguished entrepreneur and philanthropist, Harry P. Hynds, and designed by the venerable
Wyoming architect, William R. Dubois, to utilize the most then-modern structural and aesthetic
elements of the era; and
WHEREAS, after Hynds and Dubois completed the building’s construction in 1922, the
Hynds was utilized for various commercial purposes for approximately six (6) decades; since that
time, however, the Hynds has remained unoccupied for more than three (3) decades, despite its
landmark status, historic importance, premier location, and keystone value to the vitality of City of
Cheyenne’s downtown core; and
WHEREAS, given that the redevelopment of the Hynds is an essential element in the
revitalization ofthe City’s downtown core, the City, the Downtown Development Authority (DDA),
the Downtown Development Foundation, and the Cheyenne-Laramie County Corporation for
Economic Development (LEADS), altogether the Team, are mutually desirous of and committed
to the redevelopment of the Hynds, and further acknowledge that to date, all purely private sector
interest has been unfruitful absent sufficient public financial support; and
WHEREAS, the City is committed to working with its Team of economic development
partners, and a prospective developer to be chosen from responses to a request for qualifications
(RFQ) and subsequent request for proposals (RFP) prepared by the DDA, for undertaking the
redevelopment of the Hynds and the Hole as one project (the Project), to overcome any remaining
hurdles to the redevelopment, and thereby to ensure that the building becomes a beacon for further
future economic development in the downtown core area; and
WFWREAS, an essential requirement of this redevelopment project is that potential
occupants have available off-street parking, and the City is committed to working with the Team
and the selected developer to meet this requirement in whatever way the parties determine best and
most equitably meets the needs of such potential tenants, the developer, the parties, and the
community at large; and
WHEREAS, in an effort to develop a solution to meet this requirement, LEADS has acquired
the vacant property adjacent to and immediately west ofthe Hynds, such property being commonly
referred as the Hole, for the stated purpose of utilizing such property as a parking facility for
potential Hynds Building occupants; and
WI-WREAS, to demonstrate the City’s support of the Project, the City is willing to reiterate
and restate its prior commitment to contribute up to Seven Hundred Fifty Thousand Dollars
($750,000.00) to the DDA to assist with subsidizing the Project in whatever ways the DDA deems
appropriate to support the Project, and which the City deems to meet legal requirements. The City
funds would not be appropriated until the 2019 fiscal year and would not be spent unless and until
the DDA selects a fmancially sound developer to who contractually commits to the Project; and
1

WHEREAS, in connection with the construction of the parking facility, the City, its
economic development partners, and the developer acknowledge that various other agreements
related to easements, shared walls, parking use, and other logistical items may need to be negotiated
prior to the construction of the Project, but such details are beyond the scope of the current
resolution; and
WHEREAS, on May 17, 2018, the DDA Board at its regular monthly meeting took the bold
step to approve what has become known as the H2 Project involving, in part, the DDA acquiring
assignable options to purchase on the Hynds and the Hole, preparing an RFQIRFP, soliciting interest
from developers on a national level, and then with the Team, select the best developer and proposed
proposal to proceed with the Project.
The DDA conditioned the 112 project on the City’s
commitment to this resolution; and
WHEREAS, such cooperative efforts by and through the City, its economic development
partners, and the developer responsible for the 112 Project have the potential to restore the Hynds
Building to its landmark status as a premier property in the downtown business core, thereby serving
a keystone function for further economic and commercial development ofthe downtown business
core area.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City is willing to reaffirm its commitment to:
1.
Grant the DDA Seven Hundred Fifty Thousand Dollars ($750,000.00) for the Project
allocated in whatever legal way the DDA deems necessary to attract a developer through the
RFQ/RFP process for the Hynds and the Hole, consistent with the City UDC and Planning
Department requirements on this CBD-zoned Project area; and
2.
Negotiate in good faith to reach any ancillary agreements including, but not limited
to, agreements for necessary easements, shared walls, parking use, and other logistical items that
the parties require prior to redevelopment of the Hynds and the Hole; and
3.
Require, as part of the developer requirements for the proposed Project, that the
successfiil proposer provide office space on the ground floor ofthe Hynds and parking sufficient for
the DDA at no cost for a minimum of five (5) years. The DDA’s lease on its current office expires
in 2019.
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this
____
day of
,2018.
Marian J. Orr, Mayor
(SEAL)
Attest:
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk
2
